Domains. A logarithm of a retained fraction is taken only where that fraction is positive: ℎ𝑉 needs
𝑆𝑉 positive and differentiable, and Λ𝑉 needs both 𝑆ext and 𝑆emb established and positive. The difference
𝑆emb − 𝑆ext needs both established and makes no demand on their signs. An embedded arm that was not
run supplies none of these, and none of them becomes a registered quantity by appearing in this document.

5.7   What the persistence mathematics does not say
It does not say that a kept property is a good one, that a system obeying Law IV, the ARC Persistence
Law, is safe, or that embedded correction is superior, sufficient or safe (registration version 1.102, line
1087). It does not identify 𝑐 𝑉 and 𝑏 𝑉 from one excision, which returns only their difference. It certifies

                                                         23
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


kernel membership only for the accepted dynamics satisfying the premises of Theorem IV-G, 𝑠0 in
𝐾𝑉 (𝐻; 𝑇acc , 𝜀𝑉 ); for a planner it certifies only that the selected plan is safe (Theorem IV-H) and, under
Corollary IV-H′ , that the executed path stays in Ω𝑉 , never that 𝑠0 lies in 𝐾𝑉 (𝐻; 𝑇plan , 𝜀𝑉 ), which premise
(H1) excludes by requiring a feasible exit plan; and it certifies nothing for every physically possible
transformation. Related coupled-correction, survival and Paper VIII (Eastwood 2026d) results are not
deciding evidence for P15. Nothing in this section has been measured.
     Theorems IV-G and IV-H concern a system that chooses its own modifications from a declared set, by
an acceptance rule or a planner. They do not reach later training by gradient steps, the stress P15 names:
for a process that executes the step it computes, premise (G2) on its evaluated set says no more than the
theorem’s conclusion, and for a process that can take every small step that raises its objective, (G2) holds
at the boundary of the acceptable set only where the objective’s gradient points along the property’s, the
case premise (G0) excludes. For a flow, what keeps a set invariant is a condition on its boundary alone,
that the flow nowhere points out of the set (for a locally Lipschitz flow and a smooth boundary; the setting
of set invariance, Section 2), which is weaker than IV-G’s margin; the training model of Section 5.9 is the
object that bears on P15’s stress.

5.8   Kill condition K-IV
Stated before any data, in terms decidable by data yet to be collected, and consistent with P15 as registered.
Version 1.103 fixed the ARC Persistence Law’s kill condition and verdict rule (registration version 1.105,
Law IV, kill condition and verdict rule): the retention limb takes P15’s own verdict, read against a baseline
arm the deciding unit names in its own registration before any outcome and only where the installed gain
is established above a floor fixed in that registration; wherever a term of the retained fraction is judged
by a person or by a model, the scoring gate holds the limb’s reading at untested until that gate passes;
the comparison limb is refuted by the refuter its entry registers; and Law IV is SUPPORTED only when
both limbs are supported, REFUTED when either limb’s own verdict is refuted, and in every other case
UNDER TEST, with each limb’s verdict printed beside the law’s word. From version 1.105 that entry
is P23, whose refuter is “an interval on the embedded arm’s retained fraction less the external arm’s,
under later training of the same kind, at the matched budget, at a common installed gain and against the
concurrent baseline, lying wholly below the margin on each of two runs” (registration version 1.105, P23).
Version 1.104 adds readings of P15’s decision requirement and changes no law’s statement: “For P15,
the retention limb of Law IV, it widens what its scope’s automated researcher admits, for P15 alone; it
reads which arm P15’s verdict is read on, so that a unit without the embedded arm may decide P15 while
placement and Law IV’s comparison limb still need that arm; it requires a replicated run before either
support or refutation is banked; and it reads the attestation for supervised later training.” (registration
version 1.104, successor status). Item 1 below is P15’s registered refuter with its budget requirement.
Item 5 quotes the registration’s weaker-neighbour rule and says what this document takes from it. Items 2,
3 and 4 are clauses this document wrote beyond P15’s registered scoring, which they leave untouched:
item 2 is on the record and not in force, and version 1.103’s rule takes its place; items 3 and 4 carry the
label (the report’s own models; not registered).
   1. The registered face. The observation: from a deciding unit of the kind the registration specifies
      (registration version 1.102, line 1089), run under the author’s pre-fixed budget measure, an interval
      on 𝑆ext (1), the retained fraction of the externally installed gain after matched capability training
      without embedded correction, lying entirely above one half. That the baseline arm be named before
      any outcome, and that the installed gain be established above a floor fixed in advance, were this
      document’s requirements in its earlier versions (Section 5.6 and Section 10, item 8); version 1.103
      registers both for the retention limb. At its line 1089 version 1.102 fixes the budget and requires the


                                                      24
                                  The mathematics of Laws IV and V


   attestation, and it names no baseline arm; nothing here is added to P15’s frozen refuter. No upper
   cap: an interval from 0.9 to 1.3 qualifies; an interval from 0.45 to 0.9 does not; a point estimate
   does not. An interval whose lower end lies inside the at-the-margin band the instrument fixed
   (registration version 1.102, line 391) is reported as a result at the margin and not as a verdict. That
   observation refutes P15 as registered (registration version 1.102, line 1083), once it is replicated as
   version 1.104 requires, is scored for P15 exactly as the registration scores it, refutes the retention
   limb and with it Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, and takes with it what registration version
   1.102, line 1085 names (quoted in Section 5.6). As a verdict it fires exactly on intervals whose
   lower end lies above the at-the-margin band the instrument fixed around one half, every point of
   which therefore lies strictly above one half, and at every such point the retention limb’s content,
   𝑆ext (1) < 1/2, is false; an interval whose lower end is exactly one half also shows that content false
   throughout and does not fire (Section 5.6).
2. The law-level withdrawal (set out by version 0.2.1; not in force; version 1.103’s rule takes its place).
   Version 0.2.1 set out a convention: where a P15 refutation also met four evaluability controls
   fixed before any data (the baseline fixed in advance, with the convention named; the installed
   effect established by the lower end of the interval on 𝑌𝑉 (0) being at least 𝑌min ; the capability
   rise established on a separate instrument by an interval lying entirely above the minimum that
   instrument registers; and the attestation of no alignment-graded component in the subsequent
   training, registration version 1.102, line 1089), Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, would be
   withdrawn whole and Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, with it. The author did not adopt that
   convention, and it is not in force. Version 1.103 fixed what a P15 refutation does instead: it refutes
   the retention limb and so Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, read against a baseline named in
   advance, above a floor fixed in advance and under the attestation, and it leaves Law V, the ARC
   Embedding Law, to its own limbs (registration version 1.105, Law IV, kill condition and verdict rule,
   and what a refuted Law IV leaves standing). It refutes no theorem: Theorems IV-G and IV-H are
   conditional mathematics and survive, as item 5 says. It decides nothing about the comparison limb,
   whose entry, with its endpoint and refuter, is P23 (Section 5.1). Of the four controls, the capability
   rise established on a separate instrument is not among the registered conditions (registration version
   1.105, Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, kill condition and verdict rule) and keeps the label (the
   report’s own models; not registered), a requirement this document sets out for the instrument that
   decides P15; for P23, version 1.105 makes a rise of at least a ninth on the external and the baseline
   arm, read on a separate instrument, a condition of any verdict (registration version 1.105, P23, its
   fourth case of NOT EVALUABLE). No scoring code may apply the former default.
3. The battery-relative kernel kill (the report’s own models; not registered; not law content until
   registered). For a registered system, property battery, tolerance and finite battery of transformations,
   a path of the battery whose end state is established outside Ω𝑉 by the interval rule of Section 5.2
   within 𝐻 refutes the claim that the system’s start lies in the battery-relative kernel; a path produced
   by a transformation outside the battery is a coverage failure and refutes nothing; a path whose
   membership is NOT EVALUABLE decides nothing. The mechanism claims built on IV-G and
   IV-H are refuted by the observations of Sections 5.4 and 5.5 (an evaluated exit with non-negative
   total objective change; a found exit plan at or above a registered upper bound on the safe supremum)
   and are not refuted by coverage failures.
4. What is not a refutation of Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law (set out by this document in the
   registration’s own terms, registration version 1.102, line 121): an instrument that fails to run or is
   too small; an interval touching one half; a result on an unregistered budget scale; a result without
   the attestation, for the law-level reading; a bypass outside the registered battery; and any result
   about whether the system’s later conduct is good, which the theory’s content neutrality places


                                                  25
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


      outside every law (registration version 1.102, line 75); the method’s own line (registration version
      1.102, line 61: “no result about a system’s later conduct supports or refutes the method by itself”)
      makes the same exclusion for the Eden Protocol, and P15’s own endpoint, a measurement after later
      training, is not a result about conduct. Version 1.103 registers that none of the following refutes
      Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law: an instrument that fails to run or is too weak to find the effect; an
      interval touching one half; a result at the margin; a result on a budget not fixed before the outcome,
      or without the attestation P15’s entry requires; and any result about whether a system’s later conduct
      is good (registration version 1.105, Law IV, kill condition and verdict rule). That list carries this
      item’s entries except the bypass outside the registered battery, a matter of the unregistered kernel,
      and adds the result at the margin.
   5. The weaker neighbour (registration version 1.102, line 125: “A REFUTED PROPOSITION
      DEGRADES TO A NAMED WEAKER NEIGHBOUR RATHER THAN VANISHING.”). What
      survives if Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, is refuted: Theorems IV-G and IV-H as conditional
      mathematics; the verdicts on P12 and P21 as measured; and the persistence questions the registration
      keeps open in another context, where the conversion claim fails: “Even then the persistence registers
      stand, because whether early formation outlasts later enforcement is a question about durability and
      not about scaling, and it would remain open and worth answering.” (registration version 1.102,
      line 115), which is quoted for its wording of the question, not as a rule about the ARC Persistence
      Law’s refutation. Version 1.103 registers what a refuted Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, leaves
      standing: P15 keeps the mark it earned, and so do P12 and P21, and Law V, the ARC Embedding
      Law, falls with Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, only where the comparison limb’s own verdict is
      refuted; from version 1.105 P23 keeps the mark it earned as well, and the verdict that strikes Law V,
      the ARC Embedding Law, is a refuted P23 (registration version 1.105, Law IV, what a refuted
      Law IV leaves standing, and the dependence’s entry).

5.9   A minimal training model (the report’s own models; not registered)
P15’s stress is later capability training by gradient steps, which Theorems IV-G and IV-H do not reach
(Section 5.7). This section states the smallest model of that stress in which retention can be computed,
and in it gives the title’s first question an answer: an installed property fades at the rates the later loss’s
curvature sets along the directions in which it lies, and is kept in the directions that loss leaves flat. Every
result holds inside the model.

The model. A system is a point 𝜔 in a space of parameters. Later capability training is gradient flow on a
quadratic loss with one fixed curvature matrix Φ, symmetric and positive semi-definite, and minimiser 𝜔★:
𝜔¤ = −Φ(𝜔 − 𝜔★), with 𝜏 ≥ 0 its time. The property score is affine near the path, 𝑉 (𝜔) = 𝑔 ⊤ 𝜔 + const.
Two runs undergo the same later training: the base run from 𝜔0 and the treated run from 𝜔0 + 𝑤, where
𝑤 is the displacement an installation made, with installed effect 𝑌 (0) = 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑤 > 0. Under the concurrent
baseline of Section 5.6 the installed effect at time 𝜏 is 𝑌 (𝜏), the treated run’s score less the base run’s,
and the retained fraction is 𝑆(𝜏) = 𝑌 (𝜏)/𝑌 (0). The eigenvalues of Φ are 𝜆𝑖 ≥ 0, and 𝑔𝑖 and 𝑤 𝑖 are the
components of 𝑔 and 𝑤 along its 𝑖-th eigenvector. Both starting points lie in one quadratic region of the
later loss, with one curvature along the whole path, and nothing in the model fixes the flow time to which
a matched budget corresponds.
Theorem IV-S (the retention spectrum; the report’s own models, not registered).

                   𝑆(𝜏) = 𝑖 𝜛𝑖 𝑒 −𝜆𝑖 𝜏 ,     𝜛𝑖 := 𝑔𝑖 𝑤 𝑖 /(𝑔 ⊤ 𝑤),     𝑖 𝜛𝑖 = 1.                           (8)
                           ∑︁                                        ∑︁

(Proof: Appendix A.7.)

                                                      26
                                       The mathematics of Laws IV and V


    The later optimum does not enter. A part of the gain lying in a curved direction decays at that
direction’s rate, a part lying in a flat direction (𝜆𝑖 = 0) is kept, and weights of mixed sign can carry 𝑆
outside the interval from zero to one. The mode-by-mode relaxation, and its limit under training run to
convergence, are standard for gradient descent on a quadratic loss (Advani and Saxe 2017; Evron et al.
2022); Section 2 says what each work states.

The external arm never falls below its flat share. Post-training on the property alone, the registered
external arm, moves the system along 𝑔, so its weights are 𝜛𝑖 = 𝑔𝑖2 /|𝑔| 2 ≥ 0 and 𝑆ext (𝜏) ≥ 𝜛flat at every 𝜏,
where 𝜛flat is the share of |𝑔| 2 in the null space of Φ. Where that share is one half or more, the retention
limb’s content, 𝑆ext (1) < 1/2, fails at every matched budget, whatever flow time the budget corresponds
to; where it is less, 𝑆ext falls below one half beyond a finite time. In the model the retention limb is
therefore a statement about the flat share of the property’s direction and about the map from budget to
flow time, neither of which the model fixes; and the flat directions are where terms beyond the second
order decide what happens, so the model is least reliable there.

Two installations. The embedded installation is one candidate reading of “with correction embedded in
the training objective”, which P23’s entry fixes operationally: the installing examples enter the objective
under which capability is formed, from its first step and at a fixed share, up to the installation point
(registration version 1.105, P23, its first fixed thing). In the model the property is raised while the original
capability loss, with curvature Φ0 and eigenvalues 𝜆0𝑖 about 𝜔0 , stays in the objective, a local reading of
that definition and not the definition. Gradient flow for a joint time 𝜏j on that loss with a positive weight
on 𝑉 moves the system by a displacement proportional to (𝐼 − 𝑒 −Φ0 𝜏j )Φ0−1 𝑔 (Appendix A.8); as 𝜏j grows,
with Φ0 positive definite, it tends to the displacement that installs a given effect at least capability cost,
𝑤 emb = 𝑌 (0) Φ0−1 𝑔/(𝑔 ⊤ Φ0−1 𝑔).
Proposition IV-L (the cost of the two installations; the report’s own models, not registered). Measured by
the original loss at second order, the capability cost of installing an effect 𝑌 (0) is 12 𝑌 (0) 2 (𝑔 ⊤ Φ0 𝑔)/|𝑔| 4
for the external installation and 12 𝑌 (0) 2 /(𝑔 ⊤ Φ0−1 𝑔) for the embedded one, and the second is never the
larger. (Proof: Appendix A.8.)
    The cost is the second-order form Mirzadeh et al. (2020) use for forgetting, and the least-cost
displacement under one linear constraint is the Lagrangian solution Hassibi and Stork (1992) give for a
constraint on one weight (Section 2).
Assumption S (later training of the same kind). Φ and Φ0 share eigenvectors, and their eigenvalues are
similarly ordered on the directions the property depends on: (𝜆0𝑖 − 𝜆0𝑖 ′ ) (𝜆𝑖 − 𝜆𝑖 ′ ) ≥ 0 for every two such
directions 𝑖 and 𝑖 ′ . Where the original loss is stiffer along one of them than along another, the later loss is
at least as stiff along the first. Φ = Φ0 (more training on the same loss) is the purest case; Φ = 𝑐 Φ0 with
𝑐 > 0, and Φ0 plus a common multiple of the identity (the same loss with weight decay), are others.
Theorem IV-C (the comparison, under Assumption S; the report’s own models, not registered). Under
Assumption S, with Φ0 positive definite on the directions the property depends on and the two installed
effects matched, 𝑆emb (𝜏) ≥ 𝑆ext (𝜏) at every 𝜏 ≥ 0, with equality at 𝜏 > 0 exactly when 𝜆0𝑖 or 𝜆𝑖 is constant
on those directions; and the ratio 𝑆emb (𝜏)/𝑆ext (𝜏) does not fall as 𝜏 grows. (Proof: Appendix A.9.)
Corollary IV-C′ (joint training stopped at a finite time; the report’s own models, not registered). Under
Assumption S, an installation by joint training stopped at any time 𝜏j > 0 also retains at least as large a
fraction as the external one, at every 𝜏, with no condition on Φ0 beyond Assumption S; and its retained
fraction does not fall as 𝜏j lengthens, tending to 𝑆ext as 𝜏j tends to zero and, where Φ0 is positive definite
on the property’s directions, to 𝑆emb as 𝜏j grows. (Proof: Appendix A.10.)

                                                        27
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


     In words: under Assumption S, later training of the same kind, a gain installed with the capability
loss in the objective retains at least as large a fraction as one installed by external post-training, and the
ratio of the two does not fall with training; where the later loss’s curvature is unrelated to the original’s,
the embedded installation can retain less. An oppositely ordered later curvature reverses the inequality,
and where the two eigenbases differ the embedded weights can take either sign, so that what was installed
can turn into a deficit. So the model predicts a moderator: a change of capability domain that reorders
curvature along the property’s directions can undo the embedded advantage, and a unit that does not
report which case its later training was in cannot be read against it. Of the two installations, the one that
costs less capability is, under Assumption S, also the one later training erodes less; the cost ordering holds
always, the retention ordering only under Assumption S. A worked case: two directions with curvatures
4 and 1/4, Φ = Φ0 and 𝑔 = (1, 1) in that basis; at 𝜏 = 1 the external installation retains 0.399 and the
embedded one 0.734, the external having cost 4.5 times as much capability; at 𝜏 = 1/2 the external figure
is 0.509, so which side of one half it falls on depends on the flow time the model leaves unfixed; and with
the later curvatures in the opposite order, 1/4 and 4, the embedded installation retains 0.063 at 𝜏 = 1. A
weight decay towards a reference common to both runs adds the same constant to every eigenvalue of Φ,
keeps Assumption S, and multiplies both retained fractions by one factor (Section 9).

The ordering is weak, and P23’s margin is not a theorem. Theorem IV-C orders the two arms only
weakly. Where the original or the later curvature is the same in every direction the property depends
on, the two retained fractions are equal at every 𝜏; where both curvatures are 𝜆 times the identity, both
fractions are 𝑒 −𝜆𝜏 , 0.368 at 𝜆𝜏 = 1, and the model gives no advantage at all. P23 predicts more: an
advantage of at least 0.10 at the matched budget, decided only under later training of the same kind as
the capability formation, which its deciding unit records as the same loss on data of the same domain
(registration version 1.105, P23, its third fixed thing and the later training’s kind). That margin is the
registration’s declared convention, “not derived from the framework”, so P23 is an empirical prediction
and no result of this section proves it. The registration gives Assumption S and Theorem IV-C as its
reason for that narrowing, and both stay unregistered.

Coupling. Let the later objective depend on the property: the later loss plus 12 𝛾 (𝑉 (𝜔) − 𝑉tgt ) 2 , with
𝛾 > 0, so that a departure of the property from its target value costs performance. Write stf := 1/(𝑔 ⊤ Φ+ 𝑔)
where 𝑔 lies in the range of Φ, with Φ+ its pseudo-inverse, and stf := 0 where 𝑔 has a component in the
null space of Φ: the least capability cost, at second order, of lowering 𝑉 by a given amount from the later
minimum is 12 stf times the amount’s square.

Proposition IV-U (coupling; the report’s own models, not registered). For every 𝛾 > 0: (1) from the
coupled objective’s minimum, the least increase of that objective that lowers 𝑉 by a given amount is
1
2 (stf + 𝛾) times the amount’s square; (2) at that minimum the property’s distance from its target is
(𝑉 (𝜔★) − 𝑉tgt ) stf/(stf + 𝛾), which is zero where 𝑔 has a component in the null space of Φ; (3) under
further training on the coupled objective every departure of the property from its value at that minimum
decays to zero, whatever direction made it, whereas without coupling a departure made in a flat direction
stays. (Proof: Appendix A.11.)

    Part (3) is, in a model, what Creating Friendly AI (Yudkowsky 2001) says in words of a feature a
system would itself produce: “Perturb the design, and the design will swiftly return to the norm.” (section
5.3.2, page 102). There the return comes from the system’s own goals (“Every feature needs to correspond
to a subgoal in order to go on existing.”, the same page); here it comes from a coupling term in a training
objective applied by gradient flow. So in the model a property can last because little pushes on it, which is
what joint installation buys, or because the objective that drives further change pulls it back, which is what


                                                     28
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


coupling buys. Only the second restores the property after a one-off displacement, and only while the
later objective keeps the coupling term; a strong weight decay moves the equilibrium towards the decay’s
reference. Under coupling the baseline run acquires the property too, and the caution of Section 5.6
applies: the concurrent-baseline retained fraction tends to zero while the property is held.

Limitations and standing. The training model is local and quadratic, with one fixed curvature and
an affine property score; each is contested for real networks. It is offered for the conditions it names
and the measurements it suggests, never as evidence, and it supports no verdict on P15 or on P23.
Theorem IV-S is standard (Section 2); Proposition IV-L’s costs are standard in substance; Theorem IV-C
and Corollary IV-C′ are proved here. A search of 14 November 2026 (twenty sources read at source,
the nearest empirical study read in full) and a reading of 17 November 2026 of one earlier theory of
curvature-driven decay of safety did not find this comparison stated; Section 2 says what those two works
state.


6     Law V, the ARC Embedding Law
6.1     Statement
Law V, the ARC Embedding Law (added 24 September 2026; registered as a law on 15 October 2026 by
version 1.103; a law under test, Section 11) concerns the genesis strategy (register ARC-5) and carries
P12, whether build order moves the correction-leverage exponent, and P21, whether correction placed
inside the loop pulls away from correction applied outside it as depth grows. The registration (Eastwood
2026g) states it in two limbs, opening “Where correction sits is an intervention point rather than an
accident of history.” Its build-order limb reads: “a corrector formed before the capability it corrects has a
correction-leverage exponent larger, by at least the registered minimum effect, than a corrector added after
it, at matched content”; its placement limb is P21’s registered claim, word for word (registration version
1.105, Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, its statement in two limbs). Two limbs, separate hypotheses with
no shared mechanism assumed:
      • Placement limb. Its registered face is P21, verbatim (registration version 1.102, line 1219):
        “Correction that participates in each subsequent revision round outperforms correction that sees only
        the finished output, and the advantage widens as recursive depth rises rather than staying constant.”
        P21 decides an access and action-space interaction on the outcome, “never a measurement of an
        exponent” (registration version 1.102, line 1221). The registration makes the limb P21’s own
        verdict, scored under P21’s entry with the two readings it adds there: a directional reading, under
        which no widening of the registered size, or a narrowing, is scored against P21; and a mechanism
        axis, whether the monitor’s detection rate falls as the artefact it inspects grows and how much of the
        widening that fall can carry, printed beside P21’s mark and never in its place. A limb supported
        while that axis is unsupported, inconclusive or NOT EVALUABLE is printed as supported on the
        performance axis only, which is not support for the information-asymmetry mechanism (registration
        version 1.105, Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, the placement limb, and P21, the directional
        reading and the two verdicts). That mechanism axis is not the mechanism reading that follows. The
        registration’s own mechanism reading is written in its own quantities (registration version 1.102,
        line 1239): “that correction embedded in the recursion carries a larger direct depth-dependence
        than correction applied from outside it. Where that holds, the boundary rises with it”. In this
        document’s symbols the mechanism reading is 𝜂𝑜,in > 𝜂𝑜,out at matched order 𝑜. It is carried here
        as the registered mechanism reading, which no verdict in this document decides: a P21 verdict does
        not measure it, and its refutation does not prove 𝜂𝑜,in ≤ 𝜂𝑜,out .


                                                      29
                                     The mathematics of Laws IV and V


    • Build-order limb. Its registered face is P12, verbatim (registration version 1.102, line 1033): “The
      order in which a system’s components are built changes its correction-leverage exponent by at
      least the registered minimum effect.” The contrast is the registered maximal contrast (registration
      version 1.102, line 1037): “a corrector formed before the capability it corrects against a corrector
      added after it, at matched content”; the minimum effect is 0.10 on the correction-leverage exponent
      scale (registration version 1.102, line 1041); in this document’s symbols the endpoint is 𝜒first − 𝜒after
      at the placement the instrument fixes, named in the report (P12 registers no placement factor), read
      on the target axis under Section 4’s identification conditions, and on one fault set of stated type, the
      same for both orders: the registration measures the exponent “on a fixed fault set of stated type”
      (registration version 1.102, line 389), and it sets P12 beside P22 on the same quantity by different
      routes, “P22 says that elasticity differs with the type of fault corrected, and this says it moves with
      the order in which the corrector was built”, adding “The two are severable and neither carries the
      other.” (registration version 1.102, line 1045). P22 stays in ARC-3 (registration version 1.102, line
      99) and Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, does not carry it, and a build-order contrast read across
      a changed mix of fault types would carry part of the ordering P22 registers, and could reach the
      minimum effect with no effect of order at all. P12 registers no direction and is given none here.
      Version 1.103 registers the build-order limb with a direction P12 does not carry, read on P12’s
      interval and printed beside P12’s mark, never in its place (registration version 1.105, Law V, the
      ARC Embedding Law, the build-order limb). In this document’s symbols the limb’s claim is the
      genesis reading, that formation first carries the higher elasticity by at least the registered minimum
      effect,
                                                𝜒first − 𝜒after ≥ 0.10                                     (9)
      at the placement the instrument fixes (or at least the smaller minimum a future instrument registers,
      registration version 1.102, line 1041); earlier versions of this document stated it as a reading beside
      P12 and not as law content, and version 1.103 registers it as the limb (Section 9). The margin
      is the minimum-effect convention, and it stands in the reading’s statement as well as in its strike
      rule, because only so is the reading consistent with P12’s undirected registration: a reading that
      formation first exceeds formation after by a margin implies P12’s claim exactly when that margin is
      at least P12’s registered minimum, and with a smaller margin, or none, it can hold at every point of
      an interval on which P12’s registered refuter fires. So stated, the reading is the upward half of P12’s
      claim, adds a sign and nothing else, and never counts as its support a change P12’s registration
      reports “as no effect of interest rather than as no effect” (registration version 1.102, line 1041). In
      the smallest model stated here (Section 6.8), a corrector formed first out-scales one added after only
      where its coverage of the target’s faults decays, as capability is formed after it, more slowly than a
      corrector added after falls behind a target it must learn with matched content. With no co-scaling
      and a decay at least as fast as the target’s representation grows, the limb’s direction reverses. The
      limb therefore carries a premise about the persistence of what is formed first, which is the question
      of Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, asked of the corrector.

Boundary reading (conditional). Where a limb raises 𝜂𝑜𝑙 with 𝜅 > 𝜒, or raises 𝜒𝑜𝑙 with 𝜂 > 𝜁, the
burden elasticities being fixed, the balance boundary of Section 7 rises, for changes small enough, or large
changes that keep 𝜅 − 𝜒 of one sign; a change of 𝜒 across 𝜅 turns a ceiling into a floor (Proposition B-2)
(registration version 1.102, line 1045: “Where it moves upward the boundary rises with it.”; the domains
are those of Proposition B-6). The reading depends on the ARC Ceiling’s model and, through it, on
ARC-2; it is not a verdict rule, and no boundary measurement scores a limb (Section 8).

                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


6.2   Quantities, embedding defined operationally, and the prerequisite
Quantities. On a declared domain 𝐶 > 0, 𝑅 > 0, each arm’s correction service 𝑄 𝑜𝑙 (𝐶, 𝑅) is positive
and differentiable with other design factors fixed; capability 𝐶 carries the ratio scale Condition B fixes,
justified independently of the framework and fixed before any outcome (registration version 1.102, line
403), on which every exponent number in this document is stated (Section 7.7); 𝑅 is the modelled depth
where registration version 1.102, line 263 applies. Zero service is reported outside the log model and
never replaced by an outcome-chosen offset (registration version 1.102, line 265, applied to service);
because excluding zeros conditions on the outcome, the zero counts are reported per cell, and a two-part
or censored model stands among the rivals of Section 6.4. The local elasticities 𝜒𝑜𝑙 and 𝜂𝑜𝑙 are partial
derivatives of ln 𝑄 𝑜𝑙 ; the constant-elasticity case is the power law and returns the programme’s exponents
(Proposition V-3). The local reading is the author’s: his working paper of 16 August 2026 (Eastwood
2026k) defines 𝜅, 𝜁, 𝜒 and 𝜂 as local partial elasticities of 𝑊 (𝐶, 𝑟) and 𝑄(𝐶, 𝑟), and the registration
reads the growth exponent the same way (registration version 1.102, line 267: “the elasticity of capability
in depth, taken at the depth a system has reached”, with the fitted slope “the special case in which
that elasticity is constant”). Identification with the registered correction-leverage exponent needs the
conditions of Section 4; otherwise 𝜒𝑜𝑙 is the elasticity of the declared service measure. 𝜂𝑜𝑙 is the direct
depth-dependence of correction service and is not an integration fraction.

Embedding, defined operationally. Writing the next state as a function of the current state and a
correction term, against applying a correction to the finished next state, does not by itself distinguish
embedded from external correction: the second form also determines the next state and can be written as
one transition map. What can be registered is operational: the treatment schedule (whether correction
enters the next round or only inspects its output), the information available to the corrector, the action space
(accept or reject only, against generation under constraint), and whether correction feeds later revisions.
P21’s deciding unit fixes exactly these (registration version 1.102, line 1231: three regimes crossed with
depth, matched on model, calls and token budget, manipulating information access; registration version
1.102, line 1233: the action space also differs, and the per-round rejection rate is reported beside the
interaction). Matching away information access would be a new mechanism-isolating comparison, not an
amendment to P21.

The recursive-improvement condition. It is stated here (the report’s own models; not registered) as
a prerequisite beneath all five laws, in words: the outputs of one round must causally change a later
improvement transition. It is not a law; it would be a condition of the laws only if the author registers it, and
until then it is a description that no surface states as a condition of any law, Laws I to III, each a law under
test, included; no symbol is given to it here. Two operational forms have been put forward, both positivity
conditions on a mutual information, and both are declined as criteria. Both are written elsewhere with the
updater’s letter, one of the eight colliding symbols of Section 4, so they are described here in words. The
state-based form asks that the mutual information between successive states, conditional on the updater’s
current state, be positive; it is met by pure repetition. The update-based form asks that the mutual
information between the updater’s next state and the two successive states, conditional on the updater’s
current state, be positive; it separates repetition from update, but a prerequisite is a necessary condition,
and this form is not necessary: a deterministic recursion from a fixed initial state, in which every state and
every update are fixed functions of their predecessors, has zero entropy everywhere and so zero mutual
information, yet its outputs causally change each later transition. The fixed initial state is needed, and it
is a valid instance: from a uniformly random initial state the same kind of recursion can carry positive
information, one bit when that state is a single uniformly random bit. Mutual information measures



                                                       31
                                       The mathematics of Laws IV and V


statistical dependence, not the causal change the prerequisite names; neither form is an improvement
criterion either, since information can persist unchanged or guide a harmful update.

6.3     Identities and the one-path counterexample
Identity V-1 (the embedding ratio’s log-derivatives). At matched order, 𝐺 emb := 𝑄 in /𝑄 out . At fixed
𝐶: d ln 𝐺 emb /d ln 𝑅 = 𝜂in − 𝜂out . At fixed 𝑅: d ln 𝐺 emb /d ln 𝐶 = 𝜒in − 𝜒out . Along a common path 𝐶 (𝑅)
with 𝛼𝑅 = d ln 𝐶/d ln 𝑅:

                              d ln 𝐺 emb /d ln 𝑅 = 𝛼𝑅 ( 𝜒in − 𝜒out ) + (𝜂in − 𝜂out ).                         (10)

Proof: ln 𝐺 emb = ln 𝑄 in − ln 𝑄 out ; the partials are the definitions; the total derivative is the chain rule. So
the path derivative is not in general 𝜂in − 𝜂out : that equality holds at fixed 𝐶 and, along a path, only where
𝛼𝑅 ( 𝜒in − 𝜒out ) = 0, that is where capability is not moving along the path or the two arms’ capability
elasticities agree.

Identity V-2 (separate arm paths). Where the arms run on their own trajectories 𝐶in (𝑅) and 𝐶out (𝑅),
with 𝛼in := d ln 𝐶in /d ln 𝑅 and 𝛼out := d ln 𝐶out /d ln 𝑅, the depth derivative of the log-ratio is 𝛼in 𝜒in +
𝜂in − 𝛼out 𝜒out − 𝜂out , each term on its own path; further varying factors add their own chain-rule terms.

Counterexample to one-path identification. 𝑄 in = 𝐶 2 , 𝑄 out = 𝐶 on the path 𝐶 = 𝑅: the ratio equals
𝑅 and widens, d ln 𝐺 emb /d ln 𝑅 = 1, while 𝜂in − 𝜂out = 0; the widening is 𝛼𝑅 ( 𝜒in − 𝜒out ) = 1. One path
therefore does not identify the partials; crossed or off-path variation is needed (registration version 1.102,
line 621), and no power law is needed for the chain rule itself.

Proposition V-3 (constant elasticities). If 𝜒𝑜𝑙 and 𝜂𝑜𝑙 are constant on a rectangle of the (ln 𝐶, ln 𝑅) plane
containing the reference point (𝐶0 , 𝑅0 ), then on it

                       ln(𝑄 𝑜𝑙 /𝑄 0 ) = ln(𝑄 𝑜𝑙,0 /𝑄 0 ) + 𝜒𝑜𝑙 ln(𝐶/𝐶0 ) + 𝜂𝑜𝑙 ln(𝑅/𝑅0 ),                     (11)

and at matched order
                                  𝐺 emb = 𝐺 0 (𝐶/𝐶0 ) 𝜒in − 𝜒out (𝑅/𝑅0 ) 𝜂in − 𝜂out .                         (12)
(Proof: Appendix A.12.)

      This is the reduced scaling form: earned where the elasticities are constant, assumed nowhere.

6.4     The estimable model (the report’s own models; not registered)
For cell (𝑜, 𝑙), family and task, a candidate mixed model is

  ln(𝑄/𝑄 0 ) = ln(𝑄 𝑜𝑙,0 /𝑄 0 ) + 𝜒𝑜𝑙 ln(𝐶/𝐶0 ) + 𝜂𝑜𝑙 ln(𝑅/𝑅0 )
                             + 𝑢 0,family + 𝑢𝐶,family ln(𝐶/𝐶0 ) + 𝑢 𝑅,family ln(𝑅/𝑅0 )
                                                    + 𝑢 0,task + 𝑢𝐶,task ln(𝐶/𝐶0 ) + 𝑢 𝑅,task ln(𝑅/𝑅0 ) + 𝜓, (13)

with random intercepts and random slopes by family and by task, their covariance structure fixed before
any outcome, and the residual 𝜓. Where the design crosses arms within a family or a task, the random
effects also carry the arm terms (the indicators and their slope interactions), because otherwise the standard
errors of the very contrasts the kill conditions read are understated. The fixed part is saturated in the 2 × 2
cells: four intercepts, four capability slopes, four depth slopes. Equivalently, each of the three coefficient


                                                          32
                                       The mathematics of Laws IV and V


types is written with the indicators indfirst , indin and their product, and the two parametrisations are the
same twelve parameters, since the cell design has rank four. A model with random intercepts only whose
fixed part keeps six of these twelve terms (the common intercept, the intercept on the product of the two
indicators, the common slopes on ln 𝐶 and on ln 𝑅, the order × ln 𝐶 slope and the placement × ln 𝑅 slope)
omits the other six: the two indicator main effects, the placement × ln 𝐶 and order × ln 𝑅 slopes and the
two three-way slope terms. The order contrast at placement 𝑙 is 𝜒first,𝑙 − 𝜒after,𝑙 , which depends on 𝑙 unless
the three-way capability-slope term is zero, so the contrast is always reported at a named placement; the
placement contrast at order 𝑜 is 𝜂𝑜,in − 𝜂𝑜,out . Whether model family enters as a random factor or as a
fixed stratum is ruled once, before any data. A random factor needs a population of which the families are
a sample, named in the registration, and a design with a handful of families from a few providers has none;
such a design treats each family as a fixed stratum, with the family terms above as fixed effects, prints
each family’s contrast and pools the families with weights fixed in advance, as Unit 83b, the counted test
of in-loop correction with depth on three providers, pools its engines with equal weight and prints each
engine’s own estimate. The task terms are unchanged.

Estimands and controls. Any estimate of 𝜒 entering a P12 verdict is the estimate registration version
1.102, line 387 requires: a slope of one measured quantity on another carries error on both axes, the
ratio of those errors is registered before any fitting, “the attenuation-corrected estimate is the one that
enters a verdict”, and no cell contributes an exponent unless it clears the identifiability gate of “at least
four valid measurement intervals and at least one and a half decades of administered depth”. Crossed
or off-path variation in 𝐶 and 𝑅 is needed to identify the partials (registration version 1.102, line 621).
Exposure, correction compute and information access are design controls: conditioning on them defines a
resource-normalised contrast and changes the estimand; P21’s registered face is on its own registered
access manipulation (registration version 1.102, line 1231), so a matched-access design is a different
comparison (Section 6.2). Capability is treated in the same way wherever an arm can move it: where the
placement or the build order changes the capability the system attains, a comparison at fixed 𝐶 conditions
on a quantity the arm moves, so it is a controlled contrast, read as an effect of the arm only under
assumptions stated before any outcome, and the total effect, the log-ratio of the two arms’ service each
along its own path, whose depth trend Identity V-2 gives, is reported beside it; administered capability,
or target difficulty varied independently of the arm, is the preferred design factor where it can be had.
The two estimands can differ in either direction. With the capability elasticity of service constant at
𝜒: identical service surfaces give a zero contrast at every fixed 𝐶, while an arm that raises attained
capability by a factor that grows with depth has a total effect of 𝜒 times the logarithm of that factor, which
widens with depth where 𝜒 is positive; and an arm that doubles service at fixed 𝐶 while halving attained
capability has a contrast of ln 2 at every fixed 𝐶 and a total effect of (1 − 𝜒) ln 2, which is zero at 𝜒 = 1
and takes the opposite sign to the contrast where 𝜒 exceeds one. The slopes themselves are read the same
way. Where ln(𝐶/𝐶0 ) is attained capability rather than capability the design assigns, any latent factor that
moves both capability and correction service enters the slope: in a linear model with the arm assigned at
random, the slope on attained capability is the elasticity plus a term that has the sign of the product of
the latent factor’s effects on capability and on service and vanishes with it, the arm contrast at matched
capability is the arm’s direct effect less the arm’s effect on capability times that term, and the total effect is
the direct effect plus the arm’s effect on capability times the elasticity. So 𝜒𝑜𝑙 , its contrasts and 𝐺 emb read
at matched 𝐶 are identified where capability is varied by assignment (administered capability, or target
difficulty crossed with the arms) or under a no-confounding premise stated before any outcome, and not
by crossed or off-path variation in attained capability alone (registration version 1.102, line 621), which is
necessary and not sufficient; the report says whether the model’s ln(𝐶/𝐶0 ) is assigned or attained. Curved
and broken-regime rivals, a two-part or censored model for zero service, and a flexible comparator form



                                                       33
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


the closed model set; the power law must win prospectively; no selection rule is chosen here.

6.5   The registered faces, whole
P21. Its refuter, verbatim (registration version 1.102, line 1227):
      REFUTED BY: no interaction between correction placement and depth beyond the tolerance
      registered with that instrument, in a design whose own sensitivity shows an interaction of
      that size would have been detected. A design too small to find the interaction is a failed
      instrument and not a refutation, as condition D already provides.
     A constant advantage is scored against it (registration version 1.102, line 1229): “an embedded arm
that beats the external arm by the same margin at every depth is reported as a level difference and is scored
against this proposition, not for it”. A supported result establishes an access interaction, not physical or
architectural embedding (registration version 1.102, line 1223), and not an exponent (registration version
1.102, line 1221); it does not resolve the trade-off the registration states (registration version 1.102, line
1235), quoted in Section 6.7. The registration’s text gives P21’s instrument as a design draft (registration
version 1.102, line 1243: “INSTRUMENT: DESIGN DRAFT, the embedded-versus-external correction
designs. That unit’s registered sensitivity was measured on a smaller design than the one registered, and
its negative-slope hypothesis has no operating characteristic; both are recorded against that unit and are
closed before this proposition is scored.”); Section 11 states where P21’s counted test stands. A widening
advantage arriving without the registered fall in the monitor’s detection rate is reported as unexplained
(registration version 1.102, line 1233). What falls with it, verbatim (registration version 1.102, line 1237):
“FALLS WITH IT: the protocol’s engineering case, jointly with P12, P13 and P15. The three laws are
untouched, which is why it is registered separately from them.”
     On the counted instrument that has decided P21, Unit 83, the counted test of in-loop correction with
depth (Section 11), depth is the count of administered arrivals, drawn from a sealed bank and none revised
twice (registration version 1.105, P21, the deciding instrument on the counted route), so no uncorrected
fault can seed a later one, and the route by which recursion as such widens the in-round advantage
(Section 6.8) is absent by design; with the in-round detection rate and the arrivals’ fault rate unchanged
by depth, a widening there comes from how the outside arm’s detection, or its rejection of sound work,
changes with the size of what it sees (Proposition V-4(e)).
     One limit of the frozen design bears on that reading. The monitor was told to accept the entries “only
if every entry shown meets the charter for its case”, and it was shown every entry it had already accepted
together with the arriving one. A rejection removed nothing already accepted, and its score counted only
the arriving entry: one for a sound entry accepted, minus one for a faulty entry accepted, nothing for a
rejection. So once it had accepted a faulty entry, a monitor that followed its instruction would reject every
later entry, sound ones included, and each sound entry rejected scored nothing. That widens the lead
with depth without any fall in what the monitor detects: in the terms of Proposition V-4, with 𝑦 k = 0, the
outside arm’s rejection of sound work rises as depth grows. Unit 83b’s registration records a descriptive
split of that run’s widening of 0.0100 a round: 0.0024 from a fall in the monitor’s catch rate, 0.0035
from rising false alarms and 0.0041 from earlier misses, the part that arises after an accepted faulty entry
(graded there as moderate confidence). Nothing in that run is re-scored, because its registration and code
are frozen. Unit 83b, its registered successor on three providers, keeps the same prompts and scoring;
before its first call a dated addendum on its registration will name this limit and read its share on that
study’s mechanism axis.
     P12. Its refuter, verbatim (registration version 1.102, line 1035):
      REFUTED BY: a preregistered manipulation of build order producing no change in the
      correction-leverage exponent beyond the tolerance registered with that instrument, where the

                                                     34
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


      instrument has shown from its own sensitivity that a change of that size would have been
      detected. A manipulation too weak to move anything is not a refutation of this prediction; it
      is a failed instrument, and condition D above says so in advance.

    Its endpoint is the exponent on one scale, scored as a continuous claim (registration version 1.102,
line 1039: “It is a continuous claim and it is scored as one.”); the minimum effect is 0.10, and “A future
instrument may register a smaller minimum and may not register a larger one” (registration version 1.102,
line 1041); its instrument line reads NONE, “one endpoint away from an instrument” (registration version
1.102, line 1045); what would decide it is a typed extension of the correction-exponent estimator across
the maximal contrast with a joint interval (registration version 1.102, line 1049). Support is read on
that joint interval: an interval establishing a change of at least the minimum effect, in either direction,
supports P12; a change smaller than the minimum “is reported as no effect of interest rather than as no
effect” (registration version 1.102, line 1041), whatever its significance; an upward change so established
supports P12 and supports the build-order limb, a downward change so established supports P12 and
refutes the limb (Section 6.6, item 2; registration version 1.105, Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, the
build-order limb), and both verdicts are reported. A supported result “does not license any statement
about where the boundary sits, about a system built in that order being safer, or about orders outside that
contrast” (registration version 1.102, line 1047). What falls with it, verbatim (registration version 1.102,
line 1043): “FALLS WITH IT: the programme’s argument that build order is an intervention point rather
than an accident of history, and this proposition’s share of the protocol’s engineering case, which it carries
jointly with P13, P15 and P21.”

6.6   Kill condition K-V
Stated before any data, consistent with P12 (no direction) and P21 as registered. Version 1.103 fixed the
ARC Embedding Law’s kill condition and verdict rule: Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, is refuted by a
refuted build-order limb, by a refuted P21 (by its registered refuter, by a constant advantage scored against
it, or by an interval its directional reading scores against it), or by a refuted verdict on the ARC Persistence
Law’s comparison limb through the registered dependence, and by no other result; it is SUPPORTED
only when both limbs are supported and no refuted verdict on Law IV’s comparison limb strikes it; an
undecided limb never refutes it; and in every other case it is UNDER TEST, with each limb’s verdict
printed beside the law’s word and the verdict on P21’s mechanism axis beside the placement limb’s
(registration version 1.105, Law V, its kill condition, its verdict rule and its dependence on ARC-4). From
version 1.105 that refuted verdict is a refuted P23, and an inconclusive, untested or NOT EVALUABLE
P23 strikes nothing (registration version 1.105, the dependence’s entry). The items below state each
limb’s rule in this document’s terms; where a clause of an earlier version went beyond the registered
refuters, it is marked, and version 1.103’s rule stands in its place. None changes P12’s or P21’s scoring.
   1. Placement limb. Struck when P21’s registered refuter fires: in the registered design (registration
      version 1.102, line 1231), passing its sensitivity and validity gates, no interaction between placement
      and depth beyond the registered tolerance. Struck equally by a constant advantage established
      within the registered tolerance by a design meeting the sensitivity clause, which is P21’s refuter
      itself (registration version 1.102, lines 1227 and 1229). Struck also by an interval P21’s directional
      reading scores against it: an interval for the interaction lying wholly below the positive tolerance,
      in a design whose own sensitivity shows that an interaction of the tolerance’s size would have
      been detected, or an interval lying wholly below the negative tolerance, which shows a narrowing
      (registration version 1.105, P21, the directional reading). Version 1.1 of this document set out,
      as a law-level convention, a strike on an interval lying wholly on the side in which the external
      arm’s advantage widens; the registered reading takes its place. A P21 refutation strikes the limb by

   these rules; it does not measure the mechanism reading and does not prove 𝜂𝑜,in ≤ 𝜂𝑜,out . A failed

2. Build-order limb. Struck when P12’s registered refuter fires: a preregistered manipulation on
   the registered maximal contrast producing no change in the correction-leverage exponent beyond
   the registered tolerance (0.10 on the exponent scale, or the smaller minimum a future instrument

   tolerance band from −0.10 to +0.10, open at its ends, since a change of exactly the minimum is
   a change “by at least the registered minimum effect” (registration version 1.102, line 1033); an
   interval whose end falls inside the at-the-margin band the instrument fixed around the tolerance is a
   result at the margin, not a verdict (registration version 1.102, line 391). An interval establishing
   a change of at least the minimum in either direction supports P12 and the limb’s registered face.
   The limb’s directed claim, the genesis reading of Section 6.1, equation (9), is struck by one rule,
   which this document stated in advance and version 1.103 registers as the limb’s (registration version
   1.105, Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, the build-order limb): an interval on 𝜒first − 𝜒after , at the
   placement the instrument fixes and on the scale Condition B fixes (registration version 1.102, line
   403; Section 7.7), whose upper end lies below the registered minimum, 0.10, or below the smaller
   minimum a future instrument registers. The rule is the exact interval complement of the reading:
   the reading is false at every point of an interval exactly when the interval’s upper end lies below
   the minimum, and it holds at every point exactly when the lower end is at or above it. The rule
   that strikes on an interval lying wholly on the adverse side (its upper end below zero) is a special
   case of this rule, since an upper end below zero lies below the minimum; and the rule strikes the
   reading wherever P12’s registered refuter fires, since an interval inside the open band has its upper
   end below the minimum, as the reading’s implying P12 requires. An interval whose upper end
   falls inside the at-the-margin band around the minimum is a result at the margin for the reading as
   it is for P12 (registration version 1.102, line 391). The rule needs no separate sensitivity clause,
   because an interval lying wholly below the minimum itself excludes an effect of the registered
   size in the claimed direction, but a failed instrument or a missing identification of the construct is
   not cured by a narrow interval. It strikes the limb and changes nothing in P12’s mark. Wherever
   a term of P12’s exponent is judged by a person or by a model, the scoring gate holds the limb’s
   reading at untested until that gate passes (registration version 1.105, Law V, the ARC Embedding
   Law, the build-order limb). The worked cases, each read with its ends clear of the at-the-margin
   bands the instrument fixes: an interval from −0.30 to +0.05 is INCONCLUSIVE under P12’s
   registered refuter, whatever the sensitivity, because it is not inside the tolerance band and so does
   not show “no change in the correction-leverage exponent beyond the tolerance registered with that
   instrument”, and it does not support P12, because it does not establish a change of at least the
   minimum; it strikes the limb’s reading, since its upper end 0.05 lies below 0.10. An interval from
   +0.02 to +0.08, on which formation first carries the higher elasticity at every point but by less than
   the minimum at every point, strikes the reading as well, and with its sensitivity shown it fires P12’s
   registered refuter too, so the reading and P12 fall together there. P12 is read in neither direction by
   any of this. Registration version 1.104 states this limb’s refutation in two places, and they differ:
   the paragraph on the build-order limb refutes it on every interval lying wholly below the minimum

   its successor qualification). They give different verdicts on intervals such as −0.30 to +0.05, and
   on intervals inside the band from a design whose sensitivity is not shown. This report follows the
   paragraph on the limb, and version 1.105 resolves the difference the same way: the rule of the

6.7   What the embedding mathematics does not say
It does not say that embedded correction is safe, sufficient or superior on any axis but the one P21 scores.
Nor does it say that embedding a model of harm makes its use for avoidance hard to invert: such a model
is dual-use, and a small readout of it, a final projection or its sign, may be a cheap direction to change,
since Proposition IV-U’s least capability cost of lowering a property is zero along flat directions, of which
a small readout is a candidate. A supported P21 does not resolve the trade-off the registration states
(registration version 1.102, line 1235): “embedding correction in the substrate that computes maximises
the corrector’s scaling with capability and minimises its independence from the generator, because a
corrector built into the system shares the system’s blind spots by construction. Those are the two axes
named in the admissibility conditions above, and embedding moves them in opposite directions. Whether
the gain on scaling outweighs the loss on independence is not registered here and is not decided by this
proposition.” A supported P12 licenses nothing about safety (registration version 1.102, line 1047). The
identities of Section 6.3 identify no partial from one path, and the mechanism reading of registration
version 1.102, line 1239 is measured by no verdict here. Nothing in this section has been measured.

6.8 Two minimal models for Law V, the ARC Embedding Law (the report’s own models;
    not registered)
Sections 6.1 to 6.7 give Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, definitions, identities, an estimation design and
the logic of its verdicts; they derive neither limb. This section states, for each limb, the smallest model in

                                                     37
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


which it can be derived or can fail. Each model says what a test must measure, and neither is evidence for
the law.

The placement model. Revision runs in rounds 1, . . . , 𝑅, and depth is the round count. In the
propagation setting, fresh faults arrive at mean rate 𝑞 > 0 a round, and each uncorrected fault present
seeds on average 𝜎 ≥ 0 new faults in the next round, so that an uncorrected error feeds later work. The
in-round arm removes each new fault in its own round with probability 𝛿in ; the outside arm corrects
nothing during the rounds and removes each fault of the finished output with probability 𝛿out , which may
depend on the output’s size and so on 𝑅. For 𝜎 > 0 the expected faults left are

setting, one unit of work arrives in each arm each round, faulty with probability 𝑞, independently of
everything before: arrivals from a fixed bank, none revised twice, so 𝜎 = 0 by construction. The in-round
arm detects a faulty arrival with probability 𝛿in and repairs it; the outside arm sees only the register and
can only keep an arrival out, which it does to a faulty arrival with probability 𝛿out and to a sound one with

score in round 𝑅 less the outside arm’s, and keptin (𝑅) and keptout (𝑅) are the two arms’ rates of keeping
