 (f) For any estimate of the lead’s slope that is linear in the entries’ scores, such as the least-squares slope
     of the per-round means, the slope at score 𝑦 k is the slope at score zero less 𝑦 k times the estimated
     slope of keptout − keptin . This needs nothing beyond that linearity.
(Proof: Appendix A.13.)
     So the placement limb follows in the model from either of two premises and fails without them: an
uncorrected fault seeds later faults (𝜎 > 0), or the outside arm’s detection falls, or its rejection of sound
work rises, as what it inspects grows. With independent arrivals, outside rates that do not change with
depth, and an in-round detection rate and a fault rate that do not change with it either, the advantage is a
constant level, which P21 scores against; a design that reads the mechanism therefore prints the in-round
arm’s detection by depth beside the outside arm’s two rates. Part (a) says that only a per-round endpoint
tells a widening from an accumulated level; P21’s counted endpoint is per round (Section 6.5). Part (c)
says that the scale of the endpoint can decide its sign: with 𝜎 = 0.3, 𝛿in = 0.5, 𝛿out = 0.8 and 𝑞 = 1,
adv(𝑅) falls from −0.30 at depth one to −2.09 at depth eight, rises from depth nine and is first positive,
+0.36, at depth twelve, with 𝑅turn = 8.47, while the ratio rises at every depth. A difference endpoint read
below 𝑅turn gives the interaction the wrong sign, so a design that means to test recursion as such fixes
its endpoint’s scale and its depth range with 𝑅turn in view. Part (f) is a data identity, and it bears on any
counted design that scores an entry kept out. On the counted instrument that has decided P21, 𝜎 = 0 by
construction (Section 6.5).

The build-order model. A ladder of targets is indexed by capability 𝐶 ≥ 𝐶0 , each formed by capability
training from 𝐶0 . A corrector’s service on a target is 𝑄 = mis(𝐶) cvg, with mis(𝐶) the misalignment
present and cvg ∈ [0, 1] the corrector’s coverage of the target’s faults; the misalignment present is
taken to be the same under both orders at one target, so that it cancels in the contrast (the cancellation
premise). Two correctors carry the same content, 𝑛ex > 0 examples. The corrector added after is formed
against the finished target, and by the learning premise its coverage falls as the target’s representation
grows: cvgafter (𝐶) = 𝑛ex /(𝑛ex + rep(𝐶)) with rep(𝐶) = rep(𝐶0 ) (𝐶/𝐶0 ) 𝑒rep and 𝑒 rep ≥ 0, the shape of
a linear read-out learned from 𝑛ex examples in a representation of size rep. The corrector formed first
is formed at 𝐶0 with the same content, so the two coverages agree there, and is carried through the
formation of capability after it. By the decay premise its coverage is multiplied by a retention factor
ret(𝐶), with ret(𝐶0 ) = 1 and decay elasticity ℎfirst := −d ln ret/d ln 𝐶 ≥ 0, which the erosion of its own
installed function under later capability training (the object of Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, where
the corrector is part of the target) or the drift of the target’s faults away from what it learned (where it
is separate) can make positive. By the co-scaling premise, where it reads the target’s own improving
representation, its coverage also gains a factor (𝐶/𝐶0 ) 𝑒co with 𝑒 co ≥ 0; the premise holds only on the range
of capability on which that coverage stays at most one, which with constant elasticities and 𝑒 co > ℎfirst is
𝐶/𝐶0 ≤ [(𝑛ex + rep(𝐶0 ))/𝑛ex ] 1/(𝑒co −ℎfirst ) . The exponents are 𝜒first and 𝜒after , the capability elasticities of
the two correctors’ service.
Proposition V-5 (the build-order model; the report’s own models, not registered). In the build-order
model:
(a) Locally,
                                                                      rep(𝐶)
                                   𝜒first − 𝜒after = 𝑒 co − ℎfirst + 𝑒 rep      ;                     (16)
                                                                   𝑛ex + rep(𝐶)
    with constant elasticities, the estimate of the contrast across two rungs 𝐶1 < 𝐶2 lies in the closed
    interval from 𝑒 co − ℎfirst to 𝑒 co − ℎfirst + 𝑒 rep .
(b) Where ℎfirst ≤ 𝑒 co the contrast is non-negative, and it reaches P12’s registered minimum effect where
    the right side of equation (16) is at least 0.10.

                                                           39
                                     The mathematics of Laws IV and V


(c) Where ℎfirst ≥ 𝑒 co + 𝑒 rep , the contrast and every two-rung estimate of it are at most zero, and negative
     where ℎfirst > 𝑒 co : the corrector formed first has the smaller exponent, the reverse of the limb.
(d) Where log-capability rises at the rate d ln 𝐶/d𝜏 in the time 𝜏 of later training and the formed-first
     corrector keeps the fraction 𝑆(𝜏) of its installed function, ret(𝐶) = 𝑆(𝜏(𝐶)), the decay elasticity is
     ℎfirst = ℎ(𝜏)/(d ln 𝐶/d𝜏), with ℎ(𝜏) := −d ln 𝑆/d𝜏 the log-decay rate of Section 5.6 written in time
     rather than budget.
(e) Under Assumption S, an early corrector installed with the capability loss in its objective decays
     no faster than one installed externally, by the ratio statement of Theorem IV-C, so with the other
     premises the same its build-order contrast is at least as large.
 (f) A refuted comparison limb of Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, leaves the sign of the contrast
     open, since the contrast depends on the level of decay and not on the comparison; and a supported
     build-order limb says nothing about that comparison limb.
(Proof: Appendix A.14.)
    So build order alone does not decide the sign of the build-order contrast: it is the late corrector’s
learning penalty, plus any co-scaling, less the decay of the early corrector’s coverage as capability is
formed after it (Section 6.1 states the consequence for the limb). Parts (d) and (e) tie the size of that
decay to the persistence Section 5.9 models. Part (f) agrees with the registration, which fixes the ARC
Embedding Law’s dependence on the ARC Persistence Law’s comparison limb as “A REGISTERED
DEPENDENCE FIXED IN ADVANCE AND NOT AN ENTAILMENT” (registration version 1.105,
Law V’s dependence on ARC-4). The cancellation premise is a premise and not a finding: where the
early corrector acts while the capability is being formed, the misalignment present at the target is itself
an outcome of the order, and the contrast then mixes coverage with load. An instrument for P12 that
measures each corrector’s coverage on a standard injected load keeps the contrast the model’s.

Standing. Both models were stated, and read a second time within the programme, on 17 November
2026; that is not an independent check. A search of 17 November 2026, twenty-two queries in two
families, half of them for the opposite (on OpenAlex and the web, since arXiv’s query interface refused
automated requests that day), with twelve works read at their official records, each at its abstract, found
earlier statements bearing on the premises of both models, for and against them (Section 2), and found
neither model’s results stated in what was read.


7     The balance identity and Law III, the ARC Ceiling, unchanged
7.1   Identity B-1 (the local balance identity)
Let 𝑄 and 𝑊 be positive differentiable correction service and newly generated burden on one clock, in
comparable units with any conversion fixed before outcomes, on the same domain as Section 6.2. Along a
specified path 𝐶 (𝑅), with other factors fixed,

                            Δbal := d ln(𝑄/𝑊)/d ln 𝑅 = 𝛼𝑅 ( 𝜒 − 𝜅) + (𝜂 − 𝜁).                             (17)

    Proof: subtract the chain rules for ln 𝑄 and ln 𝑊 along the path. Every elasticity is local; no
power law is assumed. Δbal is the programme’s balance exponent read locally, and the registration’s
Δbalance = 𝜙𝑄 − 𝜙𝑊 (Eastwood 2026g; registration version 1.102, line 619: “the balance exponent
is Delta_balance = phi_Q - phi_W: correction service minus burden”); positive means correction is
gaining on burden. The registration’s elasticities are log-derivatives “with respect to the resource clock”
(registration version 1.102, line 619); Δbal above is per unit of ln 𝑅, so the two agree where the clock is

                                                     40
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


depth, and otherwise differ by the factor d ln 𝑅 divided by d ln of the clock. That factor is positive, and so
preserves the sign and the zero set, wherever depth rises with the clock at a positive rate; along a stretch
on which depth fell as the clock advanced it would be negative and would reverse the sign. The clock
condition is the author’s: the service clock need not equal the resource coordinate, but it must be fixed
within a model, and changing it changes the operational quantities.

7.2   Proposition B-2 (the balance root and the sign rule)
Proposition B-2. Where 𝜅 differs from 𝜒, Δbal = 0 has the single root

                                       𝛼𝑅 = 𝛼bal := (𝜂 − 𝜁)/(𝜅 − 𝜒),                                       (18)

and identically
                                         Δbal = (𝜅 − 𝜒) (𝛼bal − 𝛼𝑅 ).                                      (19)
Hence: with 𝜅 > 𝜒 the trend is favourable exactly where 𝛼𝑅 < 𝛼bal , and 𝛼bal is a ceiling, written 𝛼crit ;
with 𝜅 < 𝜒 the inequality reverses and 𝛼bal is a floor; with 𝜅 = 𝜒 the trend is 𝜂 − 𝜁, independent of 𝛼𝑅 .

     The symbol 𝛼crit is used only in the ceiling case, so that the entry of the programme’s notation
(Eastwood 2026b), the stability ceiling, keeps its meaning; its range there, at least one, holds exactly
where 𝜂 − 𝜁 ≥ 𝜅 − 𝜒 > 0, which in the registered burden model (𝜅 = 1, 𝜁 = −1) is 𝜂 ≥ −𝜒 with 𝜒 < 1: in
the pacing case 𝜂 = −1/2 with 𝜒 = 0 gives a ceiling of one half; outside it, 𝜅 = 1/2 with 𝜒 = 1, 𝜂 = 0
and 𝜁 = −1 gives 𝛼bal = −2, a floor. A non-positive 𝛼crit with 𝜅 > 𝜒 leaves no positive growth elasticity
with a favourable trend. As 𝜒 rises to 𝜅 with 𝜂 − 𝜁 > 0 the boundary grows without bound: a correction
elasticity matching the total burden elasticity leaves no finite boundary. The zero set of Δbal , the balance
frontier, is this set of points; it depends on the path field 𝛼𝑅 as well as on the service and burden surfaces.
Once the path field is specified, Δbal is a function on the (𝐶, 𝑅) plane and its zero set is a level set of
that function: where Δbal is continuously differentiable, the set is a curve near each of its points at which
the gradient of Δbal does not vanish (the implicit function theorem), so it is generically a curve, but not
always: in the pacing case at 𝜒 = 1 with 𝜂 = −1, Δbal is zero everywhere, and at 𝜒 = 1/2 and 𝜂 = 0 with
the field 𝛼𝑅 = 1 it is one half everywhere, so the set is then the whole plane or empty. Before the field
is specified the set lies in (𝐶, 𝑅, 𝛼𝑅 ), where the derivative of Δbal in 𝛼𝑅 is 𝜒 − 𝜅, so, again where the
elasticities are continuously differentiable, it is a surface near each of its points at which 𝜅 differs from 𝜒;
it carries no symbol here and Law III, the ARC Ceiling, is unchanged (Section 9).

7.3   Corollary B-3 (the registered special case)
Corollary B-3. Under the registered premises, burden proportional to d𝐶/d𝑅 along a power-law path,
read as the programme’s notation reads the registered model, the surface 𝑊 proportional to 𝛼𝐶/𝑅 with
𝜅 = 1 and 𝜁 = −1 (one path alone does not fix the partials, Section 6.3), and correction proportional to
𝐶 𝜒 (𝜂 = 0), 𝛼crit = 1/(1 − 𝜒): the frozen Law III, the ARC Ceiling, verbatim (registration version 1.102,
line 665): “Law III, the ARC Ceiling, alpha_crit = 1 / (1 - gamma): the growth exponent is capped by the
reciprocal of the correction shortfall.”

    The registration’s letter, the one inside that quotation, and this document’s 𝜒 name the same quantity
(registration version 1.102, line 305). The mapping is a symbol mapping inside the stated pacing model
and is not permission to identify the ARC Co-Scaling Law’s 𝛽𝐶 with either: “WHETHER THE SECOND
LAW’S EXPONENT AND THE THIRD LAW’S ARE ONE QUANTITY IS NOT SETTLED HERE AND
IS RECORDED AS UNESTABLISHED BELOW, and no proposition assumes they are.” (registration
version 1.102, line 271). Law II, the ARC Co-Scaling Law, is “beta_C exceeds k” (registration version


                                                      41
                                     The mathematics of Laws IV and V


1.102, line 663). Paper X (Eastwood 2026e) defines 𝑘 by fractional growth, (d𝐶/d𝑡)/𝐶 = 𝑏𝐶 𝑘 . On
the depth clock, along a power-law path 𝐶 ∝ 𝑅 𝛼 , the fractional growth is (d𝐶/d𝑅)/𝐶 = 𝛼/𝑅, which is
proportional to 𝐶 −1/𝛼 , so 𝑘 = −1/𝛼, and the relative criterion 𝛽𝐶 > 𝑘, under which the misalignment
fraction vanishes, holds wherever 𝛽𝐶 > −1/𝛼 and does not give the ceiling. The reciprocal form comes
from Paper X’s absolute criterion, its Corollary 1: absolute misalignment stays bounded where 𝛽𝐶 ≥ 𝑘 + 1
and tends to zero where 𝛽𝐶 > 𝑘 + 1. On the depth clock these read 𝛽𝐶 ≥ 1 − 1/𝛼 and 𝛽𝐶 > 1 − 1/𝛼,
that is 𝛼 ≤ 1/(1 − 𝛽𝐶 ) and 𝛼 < 1/(1 − 𝛽𝐶 ) for 𝛼 > 0 and 𝛽𝐶 < 1. The registration’s drift exponent, the
exponent of the burden itself, is (𝛼 − 1)/𝛼 under the burden model (registration version 1.102, line 637),
which is this 𝑘 plus one, so the criterion read against it is the absolute one. A change of clock, with
depth advancing in time at a rate proportional to 𝐶 𝛿 , adds 𝛿 to both exponents and leaves their difference
unchanged, so each criterion reads the same on either clock: on that wall clock 𝑘 = 𝛿 − 1/𝛼, which equals
(𝛼 − 1)/𝛼 only where 𝛿 = 1, a cycle time proportional to 1/𝐶. The absolute criterion returns the ceiling
relation only under the identification 𝛽𝐶 = 𝜒, which the registration records as unestablished (registration
version 1.102, lines 271, 309 and 613) and which needs an intervention designed to move one while
leaving the other before either stands in for the other (registration version 1.102, line 1029). Under that
identification, and only under it, “The ceiling is the co-scaling criterion with the drift exponent fixed
by the burden model.” (registration version 1.102, line 637), and Law III, the ARC Ceiling, remains a
conditional quantitative consequence of Law II, the ARC Co-Scaling Law, under its stated identifications,
not an independent finding. Laws II and III, the ARC Co-Scaling Law and the ARC Ceiling, are each a
law under test.

7.4   The pacing family
In the pacing case (𝜅 = 1, 𝜁 = −1) with a direct depth term, 𝛼crit = (1 + 𝜂)/(1 − 𝜒), a positive ceiling for
𝜒 < 1 and 1 + 𝜂 > 0; at 𝜒 = 1 the trend is 1 + 𝜂 independent of 𝛼𝑅 ; at 𝜒 > 1 with 1 + 𝜂 ≥ 0 the trend
𝛼𝑅 ( 𝜒 − 1) + (1 + 𝜂) is positive for every positive 𝛼𝑅 , as the sum of a positive and a non-negative term. At
𝜒 = 1/2 this family gives 2(1 + 𝜂). That value is not the general boundary: 2(1 + 𝜂) is the pacing-case
value; the general boundary at 𝜒 = 1/2 is (𝜂 − 𝜁)/(𝜅 − 1/2), which equals 2(1 + 𝜂) for every 𝜂 only when
𝜅 = 1 and 𝜁 = −1; at single points the two can agree elsewhere (𝜅 = 3/2, 𝜁 = −2 and 𝜂 = 0 give two
on both sides), a coincidence at one point that does not make 2(1 + 𝜂) the general boundary. So, in the
pacing special case, at the same-class cap 𝜒 = 1/2, the boundary is 2(1 + 𝜂), and the ARC Bound, 𝛼 ≤ 2
on the relative rung, is its 𝜂 = 0 value.
     The registration’s four-quantity form (registration version 1.102, line 639: “the boundary is one plus
the difference of the two direct depth-dependences, divided by one plus the burden intensity less the
correction elasticity”) is the same boundary written with the registered model’s values subtracted: the
burden intensity of that sentence is 𝜅 − 1 (the programme’s 𝜇 𝐵 ) and its direct depth-dependence of burden
is 𝜁 + 1, not 𝜁, whose name in the programme’s notation, the direct depth-dependence of burden, is the
full depth elasticity of 𝑊 at fixed 𝐶; so (1 + 𝜂 − (𝜁 + 1))/(1 + (𝜅 − 1) − 𝜒) is (𝜂 − 𝜁)/(𝜅 − 𝜒) identically,
and the registration’s “both direct terms and the burden intensity vanish” is 𝜁 + 1 = 0 and 𝜅 − 1 = 0 with
𝜂 = 0. Its worked numbers hold: 𝜒 = 1/2 with 𝜂 = 1/2 gives three; 𝜒 = 4/5 gives five; both together
give seven and a half. Two is the relative rung’s value: it bounds whether the ratio of correction service to
burden improves. A stricter question asks whether the absolute burden a system carries stops growing.
Counted over an exposure that grows with capability itself, the same assumptions return two thirds, not
two, and cumulative burden is stricter again. The absolute rung (registration version 1.102, line 609)
subtracts the correction elasticity from two rather than from one; its derivation is the registration’s and is
not reproduced here.




                                                     42
                                        The mathematics of Laws IV and V


7.5   Proposition B-5 (varying growth)
Proposition B-5. If burden is proportional to d𝐶/d𝑅 along an arbitrary smooth increasing path, then 𝑊
is proportional to 𝛼𝑅 𝐶/𝑅 and not to 𝐶/𝑅, and with correction proportional to 𝐶 𝜒 at constant 𝜒,
                                    Δbal = 1 − 𝛼𝑅 (1 − 𝜒) − d ln 𝛼𝑅 /d ln 𝑅,                               (20)
which is registration version 1.102, line 623 in symbols (“the trend of the correction-to-burden ratio is
one, less the local elasticity multiplied by the correction shortfall, less the trend of the local elasticity
itself”).
     The last term vanishes throughout a range of depth only where 𝛼𝑅 is constant on that range, that
is only for a power law there, where Δbal = 1 − 𝛼(1 − 𝜒); at a single depth it vanishes wherever 𝛼𝑅 is
stationary, which a path that is not a power law can reach: ln(𝐶/𝐶0 ) = (ln(𝑅/𝑅0 )) 3 /3 + ln(𝑅/𝑅0 ) has
𝛼𝑅 = 1 + (ln(𝑅/𝑅0 )) 2 , stationary at 𝑅 = 𝑅0 . Otherwise the general chain rule must carry the dependence
inside 𝑊 or carry 𝛼𝑅 as an extra state variable. Merely replacing a constant 𝛼 by 𝛼𝑅 in the simplified
ceiling loses this term.

7.6   Proposition B-6 (sensitivities, the gap identity, and what an intervention does)
Proposition B-6. With the other quantities fixed,
            d𝛼bal    1            d𝛼bal    𝜂−𝜁              d𝛼bal     𝜂−𝜁                d𝛼bal     1
                  =     ;               =           ;             =−           ;               =−     .    (21)
             d𝜂     𝜅−𝜒            d𝜒     (𝜅 − 𝜒) 2          d𝜅      (𝜅 − 𝜒) 2            d𝜁      𝜅−𝜒
So raising 𝜂 raises the ceiling when 𝜅 > 𝜒, and raising 𝜒 raises it only when 𝜂 − 𝜁 > 0. In the pacing
case at fixed 𝜂, the build-order gap is
                 𝛼crit (first) − 𝛼crit (after) = ( 𝜒first − 𝜒after ) 𝛼crit (first) 𝛼crit (after)/(1 + 𝜂)   (22)
in the positive-ceiling domain. In the same pacing case, with 𝜅 = 1 and 𝜁 = −1 in both arms, where a
placement contrast moves both terms, in-loop placement raises the boundary if and only if
                                 (1 + 𝜂in )/(1 + 𝜂out ) > (1 − 𝜒in )/(1 − 𝜒out ),                          (23)
with 1 − 𝜒 > 0 and 1 + 𝜂 > 0 in both arms.
     Equation (22) reads 𝜒 as an elasticity of correction capacity. A contrast measured as misalignment
removed per pass on a fixed fault set moves the boundary through equation (22) only under a stated
removal law, which the deciding instrument names. One favourable term alone is insufficient, and an
intervention that also raises burden can lower the boundary. Outside the pacing case the comparison is
between the two arms’ balance roots, each with its own burden elasticities, and the pacing condition does
not carry over: with 𝜅 = 2 and 𝜁 = −1 in both arms, ( 𝜒, 𝜂) = (1/2, 1/5) in the loop and (2/5, 3/10)
outside it, the pacing condition holds, yet the in-loop root is 4/5 and the other 13/16, so in-loop placement
lowers the boundary. These are algebraic regimes, not safety conclusions: the trend is not the level
(registration version 1.102, line 625: “this quantity says whether the ratio is IMPROVING, never whether
it EXCEEDS ONE.”), and 𝑄/𝑊 = 𝑅/100 improves throughout 𝑅 ∈ [1, 10] while staying below one.
Trend, level, backlog and event stay distinct.
     Local elasticities throughout make this a local boundary; a boundary written as a function of the
architecture (whose letter for the architecture is one of the eight colliding symbols and is not printed; its
root reads every elasticity, the burden’s included, in the architecture) is the statement that changing the
arm changes the arm’s elasticities and so moves 𝛼bal through these sensitivities; it is written here per arm
without an architecture symbol, and where the burden elasticities also differ between arms the comparison
is the general one above.

                                                           43
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


The ARC Bound reading. The Bound is the same-class value of the pacing model at 𝜒 = 1/2 and 𝜂 = 0,
not a fourth or sixth law. A control stands: a study quoting the simple form declares the corrector’s direct
depth-dependence before the run and shows it near zero by an equivalence result with a pre-fixed band
whose upper edge is at most half the margin the deciding instrument registers for the profile maximum; a
wider band, or a missing demonstration, is NOT EVALUABLE for the Bound reading, never survival.
That margin is not yet a number: P3’s tolerance “is the author’s to fix” and until then P3’s supported
outcome and this control are NOT EVALUABLE (registration version 1.102, line 843), which Section 10
records. No numerical band is chosen here; P3’s statement, refuter (registration version 1.102, line 851)
and scoring are unchanged, and a positive 𝜂 asserted after a run never excuses a P3 exceedance.

The Boundary Transfer (the report’s own models; not registered). A held-out prediction of the
balance transition from independently estimated elasticities inherits their precision. The registration
reports the growth exponent’s re-estimate as approximately 0.49, one model’s fit, a regression estimate
with a standard error of 0.20, and the 95 per cent interval [−1.3, 2.9] as the bootstrap interval on the same
row’s endpoint estimate of 0.59 (registration version 1.102, line 207, which also calls the re-estimate
robust, a word version 1.104 drops); that interval’s positive part contains 𝛼crit = 2, on the relative rung,
at the registered dials, and Δbal is positive at either point estimate and negative at 2.9, so no transfer
prediction built on it has a decided sign; the non-positive part of the interval lies outside the burden model,
whose burden must be positive. A transfer unit therefore carries its own precision gate, fixed before the
transition data are opened: the half-width of the predicted boundary’s interval small against the band the
boundary-mapping unit registers for the boundary location, the registered standard error of its estimator
(registration version 1.102, lines 621 and 633); it is an unregistered study, not law content.

7.7   Proposition B-7 (the capability coordinate)
Proposition B-7. Let capability be re-expressed by an increasing differentiable map whose local elasticity,
the derivative of the logarithm of the new coordinate in the logarithm of the old, is positive throughout.
Read in the new coordinate, the growth elasticity 𝛼𝑅 is multiplied by that elasticity, 𝜒 and 𝜅 are divided
by it, and 𝜂, 𝜁 and Δbal are unchanged; so 𝜅 − 𝜒 keeps its sign, 𝛼bal is multiplied by the same elasticity, the
verdict 𝛼𝑅 < 𝛼bal is unchanged, and every contrast of capability elasticities between arms at a common
capability (𝜒first − 𝜒after , 𝜒in − 𝜒out ) is divided by it. (Proof: Appendix A.16.)

     Signs, ceilings against floors and every verdict on the balance are therefore free of the coordinate,
and numbers are not: on the squared coordinate, 𝐶 2 , an order contrast of 0.15 is 0.075, below P12’s
minimum effect. The registration fixes the numbers on one scale: “Every numerical exponent and every
exponent-scale margin in this registration is stated on the scale this condition fixes and on no other”
(registration version 1.102, line 403, Condition B), and for the same-class value, “THE BOUND IS
FIXED ON ONE CAPABILITY SCALE AND ON NO OTHER” (registration version 1.102, line 935).
𝛼crit = 1/(1 − 𝜒), the ARC Bound’s two on the relative rung, the same-class value one half, P12’s
minimum effect 0.10 and the genesis reading’s margin are numbers on that scale. A meaningful zero and
an uncapped range, which the registration gives its ladder scale (registration version 1.102, line 259),
do not by themselves fix it: every positive power of 𝐶 keeps both and multiplies the growth elasticity
by the power, so the justification Condition B requires must also exclude power re-expressions. The
registered burden model’s values are themselves values on that scale (on 𝐶 2 , 𝜅 = 1 becomes one half), so
re-expressing capability alone and reapplying 1/(1 − 𝜒) shows nothing: with 𝛼 = 9/5 and 𝜒 = 9/20 the
bound is 20/11 on 𝐶; on 𝐶 2 , 𝛼 = 18/5, 𝜒 = 9/40 and 𝜅 = 1/2 give 40/11, and the verdict stands, while
1/(1 − 9/40) = 40/31 would reverse it. One inconsistency of the frozen registration bears on Law V,
the ARC Embedding Law, and is carried to the successor registration (Section 10, item 15): registration


                                                      44
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


version 1.102, line 403 states every exponent-scale margin on Condition B’s scale, but Condition B’s
lists (registration version 1.102, lines 409 and 415) leave P12 out, and Condition E2 (registration version
1.102, line 421) names P12 among the predictions that return “no correction exponent”, although P12’s
endpoint is “the quantity the third law’s ceiling is written in” (registration version 1.102, line 1039); P12’s
0.10 rests on Condition B’s scale through registration version 1.102, line 403 alone.

7.8 Proposition B-8: the balance over a finite range of depth (the report’s own models;
    not registered)
Identity B-1 gives the trend of the ratio 𝑄/𝑊 at a point. Over a range of depth it gives the change in the
ratio’s level.

Proposition B-8. On one specified trajectory, with 𝑄 and 𝑊 positive, in comparable units, on one clock
and with one conversion fixed throughout, let 0 < 𝑅1 < 𝑅2 lie in the domain and let Δbal be integrable in
ln 𝑅 between them. Then
                                                                ∫ 𝑅2
                    ln[𝑄(𝑅2 )/𝑊 (𝑅2 )] = ln[𝑄(𝑅1 )/𝑊 (𝑅1 )] +         Δbal (𝑟) d ln 𝑟,              (24)
                                                                      𝑅1

where 𝑄(𝑟) and 𝑊 (𝑟) are read along that trajectory and 𝑟 is the variable of integration. (Proof:
Appendix A.17.)

   So the level at the start of a range is part of any prediction of the level at its end, and the trend alone
predicts neither. For a constant trend Δbal other than zero, on a trajectory anchored at 𝑅0 , the ratio is
𝑄(𝑅)/𝑊 (𝑅) = [𝑄(𝑅0 )/𝑊 (𝑅0 )] (𝑅/𝑅0 ) Δbal , and it meets one at

                                      𝑅★ = 𝑅0 [𝑊 (𝑅0 )/𝑄(𝑅0 )] 1/Δbal .                                   (25)

     With a positive trend the crossing lies beyond the anchor exactly when 𝑄(𝑅0 ) < 𝑊 (𝑅0 ): where
service already exceeds burden at the anchor the expression returns a depth before it, and where the two
are equal it returns the anchor. With a negative trend the same expression gives the depth at which the ratio
falls through one, which lies beyond the anchor exactly when 𝑄(𝑅0 ) > 𝑊 (𝑅0 ). With a zero trend the ratio
keeps its starting level throughout. 𝑅★ is a formal crossing: nothing in it shows that the depth lies inside
the range measured, or inside any range a system can reach. Nor does a ratio above one bound a backlog,
a transient error or a harm; trend, level, backlog and event stay distinct (Section 7.6). A prediction made
with this proposition therefore records in advance the levels at the anchor with their uncertainty, the range
of depth it covers, the clock and the conversion, and the separately measured outcome it is meant to reach;
and none of these is chosen with the held-out observation in view.

The coordinate. Proposition B-7 governs any change of the capability coordinate, and the power case
shows the pattern: where capability is re-expressed so that its ratio to its reference value becomes (𝐶/𝐶0 ) 𝑝
with 𝑝 > 0, 𝛼𝑅 becomes 𝑝𝛼𝑅 , 𝜒 becomes 𝜒/𝑝 and 𝜅 becomes 𝜅/𝑝, while 𝜂, 𝜁 and Δbal are unchanged,
and so is the integral above. To change the coordinate and keep the old number for the burden’s capability
elasticity is to change the model (Section 7.7). Every numerical threshold on an exponent, P12’s minimum
effect among them, stays on the scale Condition B fixes. Nothing in this proposition replaces the ARC
Co-Scaling Law’s criterion, that 𝛽𝐶 exceeds 𝑘, or removes an identification on which Law III, the ARC
Ceiling, rests.

                                          The mathematics of Laws IV and V

    The claim fixes the measurement, never the reverse. P12 is read on its undirected contrast in the
correction-leverage exponent, on the registered axis. P21 is read on its behavioural outcome, the interaction
of placement with depth, within its registered access and action-space design. P15 is read on the retained
fraction of an externally installed gain after later capability training, and P23 on the difference of two
retained fractions, the embedded arm’s less the external arm’s, under later training of the same kind. A
ratio of correction service, a behavioural gain at one fixed budget, or a comparison of retained fractions
on another scale or under another kind of later training is none of these four endpoints and is never scored
in place of one. A mediation hypothesis is a candidate for a registration of its own, and it is not part of a
successor to version 1.102 until it has been reviewed and given an instrument. Version 1.103 registers
two directional readings without changing these endpoints: the ARC Embedding Law’s build-order limb
reads P12’s interval with a direction P12 does not carry, and P21’s directional reading scores no widening
of the registered size, or a narrowing, against P21 (Sections 6.1 and 6.6).

8    Dependencies, logical separation and conventions
    1. Register dependencies (the statement paper’s dependency table, Eastwood 2026h, in its section 2a):
       ARC-1’s failure does not refute the persistence registers; ARC-2 depends on ARC-1’s measurables,
       not its verdict, and its failure takes ARC-3’s derivation; ARC-3’s failure does not erase ARC-2’s own
       result. The ARC Persistence Law’s objects need no growth law; the ARC Embedding Law’s two
       limbs need none; only Law V’s boundary reading depends on the ARC Ceiling’s model and through
       it on ARC-2, and it needs the same system and admissible scopes. The registration (Eastwood
       2026g) reads that table’s rows at the width of its registered lines, narrowing the row for ARC-2 and
       the row for ARC-4 before any data (registration version 1.105, the ARC Ceiling’s word and the
       ARC Embedding Law’s dependence on ARC-4).
    2. Logical separation, at the level of verdicts. The registration states it: “The laws are untouched.”
       (registration version 1.102, line 1087, for P15) and “The three laws are untouched” (registration
       version 1.102, line 1237, for P21). The registration reads the untouched laws of P21’s entry as
       Laws I, II and III (registration version 1.105, the qualifications to the entries of P21 and P15), since
       Laws IV and V, the ARC Persistence Law and the ARC Embedding Law, are not untouched by
       failures of their own carriers, and under it a P15 refutation refutes Law IV through its retention limb
       (Section 5.8), so the laws these lines call untouched are Laws I to III. The ARC Ceiling’s relation
       holds at every value of 𝜒, so any P12 verdict, which is a contrast in the very symbol Law III’s
       equation is written in (registration version 1.102, line 1039: “the quantity the third law’s ceiling
       is written in”), is consistent with it; separation is independence of truth values, not absence of
       the symbol. Three listed models make it concrete, each at the registered dials (𝜒 = 1/2, 𝜂 = 0,
       𝜅 = 1, 𝜁 = −1) with a growth exponent of 3/2, so that the growth exponent lies below the ceiling
       of two and the balance exponent is positive, Δbal = 1/4: (a) an interval on 𝑆ext (1) from 0.85 to
       0.95, which would refute P15 and so the ARC Persistence Law’s retention limb, and with it Law IV;
       (b) an interval on the build-order change in the exponent lying inside P12’s open tolerance band,
       and an interval on the placement-by-depth interaction on the outcome lying inside the tolerance
       registered with P21’s instrument, each from a design meeting its sensitivity clause, so that both
       registered refuters would fire and both limbs of Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, would be struck;
       (c) an interval on 𝑆ext (1) from 0.2 to 0.4 with the intervals of model (b), under which Law IV’s
       retention limb would be supported, the law staying under test, while both limbs of Law V would be
       struck. Across the three the verdicts on P12, P15 and P21 vary while the dials, and every statement
       of Sections 7.1 to 7.4 about them, stay fixed. The dials do not by themselves establish Laws I to III,
       each a law under test: Law I, the ARC Principle, is a claim about measured growth, and the ARC
       Co-Scaling Law’s criterion compares 𝛽𝐶 with 𝑘, which the registration does not identify with this
       model’s elasticities (Section 7.3). Model (b) is written on the registered endpoints because P21
       decides an interaction on the outcome, “never a measurement of an exponent” (registration version
       1.102, line 1221), and its refuter reads that interaction (registration version 1.102, line 1227); read
       on the service surfaces instead, 𝜂in = 𝜂out would make 𝐺 emb constant in depth along a growing
       path only together with 𝜒in = 𝜒out (Identity V-1), and its constancy is not what fires P21’s refuter.
       These are separations of statements, not empirical findings. A countermodel put forward for the
       case in which Laws I to III hold while Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, fails takes a collapse,
       𝑆𝑉 (𝑥) = exp(−10𝑥), which on the external arm would support P15; it reads the failure of Law IV
       in the comparative sense of Section 5.6, the opposite direction from model (a), which is one more
       reason the comparative content was kept apart from P15’s face; version 1.103 registered it as the
       ARC Persistence Law’s comparison limb, without a proposition, and version 1.105 registers that
       limb’s proposition, P23 (Section 5.1). Two registered links are recorded rather than left to be found:



                                                      47
                                  The mathematics of Laws IV and V


   registration version 1.102, line 663 carries Law II, the ARC Co-Scaling Law, “by P4, with P7 asking
   what class deployed correction actually belongs to, and P12 and P15 asking whether placement
   and subsequent training move it”; and registration version 1.102, line 667 holds the ARC Ceiling’s
   relation only where “the direct depth term is zero”, which the mechanism reading 𝜂𝑜,in > 𝜂𝑜,out
   denies in at least one placement, so that where that reading holds Law III’s simple form, and the
   ARC Bound read from it, apply in at most one placement; that link is confined to the boundary
   reading and to the 𝜂 term of the Bound-reading control, and a P20 verdict is scored in ARC-3 alone.
3. The register convention (set out by version 0.2.1; not in force; version 1.103’s dependence takes its
   place). Version 0.2.1 set out, as a grouping fixed in advance and not as an entailment, that on an
   evaluable P15 refutation Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, be withdrawn whole, and Law V, the
   ARC Embedding Law, with it as a law of the theory; that Law V’s refutation not withdraw Law IV;
   and that P12 and P21 keep their own measured verdicts. Two published statements stood behind it,
   and they are different statements. The registration ties a P15 refutation to “this programme’s case
   that correction has to sit inside the system rather than be applied to it from outside” (registration
   version 1.102, line 1085). The statement paper’s dependency table has “ARC-4 falls and takes
   ARC-5 with it”, and the trigger of that fall in the same paper is the comparative observation of its kill
   sentence (Section 5.6), not P15. Nothing in 𝜂𝑜,in > 𝜂𝑜,out or in the P12 endpoint is entailed by any
   value of 𝑆ext (1). Version 1.103 fixed the dependence before any data, as a registered dependence
   and not an entailment, at the narrower of the paper’s two widths: Law V, the ARC Embedding
   Law, is struck only by a refuted verdict on the ARC Persistence Law’s comparison limb, once a
   later successor has registered that limb’s entry; a refuted P15 alone refutes Law IV and leaves
   Law V to its own limbs; until the comparison limb is registered no result on ARC-4 strikes Law V;
   and a Law V struck in this way is printed as refuted, struck by its registered dependence on that
   comparison, with the unit and the date, while P12 and P21 keep the marks they earned (registration
   version 1.105, Law V’s dependence on ARC-4). It narrows the paper’s table row and its note of 3
   October 2026, “where ARC-4 falls, ARC-5 falls with it”, to that form. Version 1.105 gives the
   dependence its entry: the refuted verdict that strikes Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, is a refuted
   P23, an inconclusive, untested or NOT EVALUABLE P23 strikes nothing, and a refuted P15 alone
   still leaves Law V to its own limbs (registration version 1.105, the dependence’s entry).
4. No P12, P15 or P21 verdict automatically refutes another numbered proposition. P19’s refutation
   removes ARC-5’s motivation and refutes nothing in it (registration version 1.102, line 111). P13
   stays outside the laws.
5. The Eden Protocol is a method, not a law, and is not entailed by these laws: “It follows from no
   law here, it is carried jointly by P12, P13, P15 and P21, with P7 as its premise and P19 as its
   motivation, and it can fail while every law stands or stand while they fall.” (registration version
   1.102, line 61); its endogeneity condition is tested by the method’s own registrations and no verdict
   here turns on it (registration version 1.102, line 675). Its success criterion is a formation criterion,
   not a later-conduct criterion. The registration reads the laws of that sentence as Laws I, II and III,
   since three of the Protocol’s four carriers now also carry Laws IV and V, the ARC Persistence Law
   and the ARC Embedding Law, and the Protocol remains a method, not a consequence of any law
   (registration version 1.105, its qualification of the method’s separability). Version 1.105 makes
   P23 a fifth carrier of the method’s endogeneity condition, with P12, P13, P15 and P21, so that if
   those five fail the Protocol’s engineering recommendation fails with them, and Laws I to III are
   untouched (registration version 1.105, a fifth carrier). The Honey Architecture (Paper VI, Eastwood
   2026c) is likewise outside every verdict here.
6. The flagship conjunction P1, P4, P16, P20, P21 stands as registered (registration version 1.102, line
   763); a surviving subset is not the conjunction.


                                                  48
                                       The mathematics of Laws IV and V


    7. A persistence advantage and a favourable elasticity contrast are separate: an architecture can
       preserve a property with no favourable contrast, or improve scaling while losing it; a positive
       retention comparison alone is not causal mediation (Section 9).


9    The register of unregistered objects
Each entry is a proposition or object this document states beyond the registered propositions, and each
carries the label the report’s own models; not registered, printed in parentheses after its name wherever it
is stated, or, in the title of a numbered result, after the name inside the title’s own parentheses, as “the
report’s own models, not registered”: the object is this report’s own mathematics, model, reporting rule
or requirement, no registration fixes it, and none is law content until the author registers it. Version
1.103 (Eastwood 2026g) registers, as the laws’ own words, the build-order limb’s direction with the
genesis reading’s strike rule, the ARC Persistence Law’s comparison limb, which names the comparison
IV-PERSIST writes, and verdict rules for both laws that take the place of the law-level clauses this
document set out; version 1.105 registers that limb’s proposition, P23, on the difference this section rules
primary (registration version 1.105, P23, its fourth fixed thing). Table 4 marks each row they reach. Each
entry carries what it needs. “The author’s” is reserved for his own records.

Ruling on the Λ𝑉 mediation bridge. Adopted (the report’s own models; not registered), with this status
line: “Mediation contrast (the report’s own models; not registered); not law content; Λ𝑉 := ln(𝑆emb /𝑆ext )
at the evaluation budget, defined only where both retentions are positive; the primary contrast is the
difference 𝑆emb (𝑥) − 𝑆ext (𝑥), which is defined wherever both retained fractions are established, whatever
their signs, with Λ𝑉 reported beside it where defined; neither is defined without the embedded arm, and
both need the measurement of Section 5.6.1; instrument: a unit registered in a scope that carries both the
retention contrast and the elasticity contrasts, since P15’s unit is governed by S5 (registration version 1.102,
line 237) and P12 and P21 by S7 (registration version 1.102, line 239), and no proposition may borrow a
scope (registration version 1.102, lines 241 and 247), so a retention contrast read on the registered two-arm
unit (registration version 1.102, line 1089) alone is an S5 quantity that bears on no ARC-5 proposition; a
positive value is not mediation: the mediating sequence stated here, embedding to the elasticity contrasts
to Δbal to a persistence advantage, has three links, each needing its own identified estimate, and each link
can fail; the adverse margin, multiplicity and registration are the author’s.” Reasons. It is adopted rather
than declined on its merits: it is the one object in the set that would connect the two registers once one
system is registered in a scope that carries both contrasts. It is stated on the difference scale first because
the log form is undefined on a region the data can reach (where one retention is negative and the other
positive, or where either is zero), and because where both retentions are negative the log form is defined
but its sign is the reverse of the difference’s. A dilution common to the two arms, such as weight decay
towards a common reference, multiplies both retained fractions by one factor: it leaves their ratio, and
Λ𝑉 , unchanged and shrinks their difference by that factor (Section 5.9). A margin fixed on the difference
therefore reads placement and common dilution together; an instrument that fixes its margin there reports
Λ𝑉 beside it, with an arm that measures the common factor. It is one object with IV-PERSIST, so that one
test does not become two propositions. Nothing states that the loop is closed. Version 1.103 registered
the ARC Persistence Law’s comparison limb, which names the comparison this contrast reads, without a
proposition, and its entry, P23, fixes the margin and the scale: the difference decides, at a margin of 0.10,
and the logarithm of the ratio, Λ𝑉 ’s value where both fractions are positive, is printed beside it and never
decides (registration version 1.105, P23, its fourth fixed thing; Section 5.1).

                                                        52
                                     The mathematics of Laws IV and V


    Two things follow for the successor registrations. A result on the difference and a result on Λ𝑉 are
one result, not two corroborations. And Λ𝑉 as a mediation contrast entered no registered version: version
1.105 registers the difference as P23’s endpoint and calls the mediation contrast unregistered, so Λ𝑉 , its
margins and any instrument for it are candidates for a registration of their own (registration version 1.105,
P23, what a supported result does not establish; Section 10, item 9).


10     Open questions
There are fifteen questions in all, numbered as version 1.1 numbered them. Where an item names a
default, that default stands until the author says otherwise, and none is chosen after a result. Version 1.103
(Eastwood 2026g), registered on 15 October 2026, has since settled items 3, 4, 5 and 10, and items 1, 2,
13, 14 and 15 in part, and version 1.105, registered on 18 November 2026, settles item 8 in part and more
of items 1 and 15; each such item says what the registration fixed and what stays open, and the version 1.1
wording of the items version 1.103 reached is kept in Appendix B.4. The other items stay open as written.
   1. The ARC Persistence Law’s content. Settled in part by version 1.103: Law IV, the ARC Persistence
      Law, has two limbs, the retention limb, P15’s registered claim word for word, and the comparison
      limb. Version 1.105 registers that limb’s entry, P23: its operational definition of correction
      embedded in the training objective, its concurrent baseline, its margin of 0.10, its scale, the
      difference, and the embedded arm’s own fraction printed and never required to clear one half,
      decided only under later training of the same kind (Section 5.1); the comparison under later training
      of another kind has no registered proposition. The loss mechanism, active removal against dilution,
      stays unresolved for real systems; the training model of Section 5.9 separates the two inside a model,
      removal acting direction by direction at rates the later loss sets and dilution acting on both arms
      alike.
   2. What a P15 refutation takes from Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law. Settled in part by version
      1.103: a refuted retention limb refutes Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, read against a baseline
      arm named in advance, above a floor fixed in advance and under the attestation, and the law is then
      printed refuted with the proposition, the unit, the date and whose run it was (Section 5.8). Of the
      four controls version 0.2.1 set out, the capability rise established on a separate instrument is not
      among the registered conditions (registration version 1.105, Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, kill
      condition and verdict rule) and stays open.
   3. The register convention. Settled by version 1.103: Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, is struck
      through Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, only by a refuted verdict on Law IV’s comparison limb,
      which from version 1.105 is a refuted P23, and a refuted P15 alone leaves Law V to its own limbs
      (Section 8, item 3).
   4. Whether Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, is severable by limb, a strict conjunction, or stands on
      the placement limb alone. Settled by version 1.103: Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, is refuted
      when either limb’s own verdict is refuted, or when its dependence strikes it, and supported only
      when both limbs are supported (Section 6.6).
   5. The axis P21’s result is read on. Settled by version 1.103, which reads the placement limb on P21’s
      own verdict, the registered access interaction and the default here, and prints P21’s mechanism axis
      beside it (Section 6.1); the service-scaling mechanism reading of registration version 1.102, line
      1239 would need a separately specified bridge and design, never a rescoring.
   6. Whether the build-order limb is scored with class left free, as P12 registers (default); holding it
      fixed would be a changed contrast.

 8. The baseline convention for the retained fraction (the concurrent baseline, the default, which needs

      rule of the paragraph on the limb the one rule (Section 6.6, item 2); it leaves P12’s listing under

11    What this paper does and does not show
Status. Laws IV and V, the ARC Persistence Law and the ARC Embedding Law, were added on 24
September 2026 and registered as laws on 15 October 2026 by version 1.103 of the theory registration
(Eastwood 2026g), each with its statement, its kill condition and its verdict rule fixed before any data
that could decide it. Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, carries P15 and P23, in register ARC-4, P23
the entry of its comparison limb; Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, carries P12 (undirected) and P21,
in register ARC-5, and reads its build-order limb on P12’s interval with a direction P12 does not carry;
P12, P15 and P21 were registered on 8 September 2026 and P23 on 18 November 2026 (Section 3.2).
Each law is a law under test. Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, is not yet tested. Law V, the ARC
Embedding Law, remains a law under test: its placement limb, P21, is supported on the performance
axis only, under the registered test, by the programme’s own run, family-dependent, with its mechanism
axis not evaluable, which is not support for the information-asymmetry mechanism, and one disclosed
recovery amendment; its build-order limb, read on P12, is undecided, since P12 is untested. That run
is Unit 83, registered on 22 October 2026 (DOI 10.17605/OSF.IO/MN73W, Eastwood 2026j) and run
on 27 and 28 October 2026 (results: DOI 10.17605/OSF.IO/98HKR). Today one limb has a registered
instrument that has run: P21, the placement limb of Law V, the ARC Embedding Law (Unit 83); a second
registered test of it, on three providers, has not yet run (Unit 83b, DOI 10.17605/OSF.IO/4HK32). P15,
P23 and P12 have no instrument, so Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, cannot yet be supported, and
the build-order limb of Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, can be neither supported nor struck. The
programme’s first registered study, Unit 06, a classification audit that tests no law, was registered on 1
October 2026 (DOI 10.17605/OSF.IO/RV2B5; Eastwood 2026i) and ran on 6 October 2026; it is reported
in full as administratively incomplete, with no verdict claimed (results: DOI 10.17605/OSF.IO/4BPHG).
Version 1.3 added the hypotheses version 1.2 left unstated, the scope of Theorems IV-G and IV-H, a
minimal training model for Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, and two minimal models for Law V,
the ARC Embedding Law. This version, 1.4, adds the premise Proposition V-4(c) needs and the range
Proposition V-4(e)’s reading needs, corrects how Section 7.3 reaches the criterion of Law II, the ARC
Co-Scaling Law, and reads the registration at version 1.105: its theorems are proved under the assumptions
each states, its identities are checked, and Section 10 lists fifteen questions, eleven of them still open in
whole or in part. Theorems IV-G, IV-H, IV-S and IV-C, Corollaries IV-G′ , IV-H′ and IV-C′ , Lemmas
IV-K and IV-M′ and Propositions IV-N, IV-R, IV-L, IV-U, V-4 and V-5 are logical proofs, not identities;
no proof assistant is used, and they rest on the written proofs, which finite models exercise and do not
replace.




                                                     55
                                     The mathematics of Laws IV and V


What kind of document this is. This document is not a registration, not an empirical result, and not
authority to run any study. Registration versions 1.100, 1.102, 1.103, 1.104 and 1.105 stay frozen byte
for byte; Laws I to III stand exactly as registered (registration version 1.102, lines 659, 663 and 665);
no proposition is added to or moved between registers; P12 keeps no direction; and every object this
document introduces beyond the registered propositions and the laws’ registered words is marked (the
report’s own models; not registered), the label Section 9 defines, and is not law content until the author
registers it. Where version 1.103 or version 1.105 registered an object this document had stated, the object
is marked as registered by that version where it stands (Section 9).

What this document is. The mathematics of Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law (register ARC-4,
carrying P15 and P23), and Law V, the ARC Embedding Law (register ARC-5, carrying P12 and P21):
the objects, the conditional theorems with proofs, the identities that connect the two laws to the registered
balance model, the registered faces quoted byte for byte, and the kill conditions. The register assignment
is that of registration version 1.102, line 99 (“ARC-4, VALUE PERSISTENCE: P15 alone.” and “ARC-5,
THE GENESIS STRATEGY: P12 and P21.”), with P23 placed in ARC-4 by version 1.105; the seven
propositions in no register are those of registration version 1.102, line 101 (P5, P7, P10, P11, P13, P14,
P19), and they stay there.

What it is not. It proves no empirical law: algebra proves consequences of assumptions, not that real
systems satisfy them. It runs no test. It adds no registered proposition and moves none. It does not restate
Laws I to III (Section 9). It supplies no missing measurement and chooses no scientific parameter the
author has held (Section 10). Section 2 names the nearest earlier work found and says what each states;
where a search was made, its date and scope stand with the result it concerns (Sections 5.9 and 6.8).

Safety and usefulness. No safety measure is stated here, so none is paired with a usefulness measure:
Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, says what persists, not that what persists is good, and Sections 5.7
and 6.7 say what the mathematics does not show.


12     Limitations
The limits below are the report’s own, each stated where it arises; they are gathered here so that none is
missed.
     • Conditional mathematics. Every theorem, lemma and proposition holds under the premises it
       states, and the report shows for no real system that those premises hold (Section 11). Nothing in
       Sections 5 to 7 has been measured.
     • Proofs and checks. The proofs are written proofs. No proof assistant is used; finite models exercise
       the proofs and do not replace them, and the computer-algebra checks of the identities and the second
       check of the working draft are the programme’s own, not independent verification (Appendix B).
     • The minimal models. The training model of Section 5.9 is local and quadratic, with one curvature
       and an affine property score, and Theorem IV-C holds only under Assumption S; the two models
       of Section 6.8 rest on the premises they name. Each says what a test must measure, and none is
       evidence for either law.
     • The margins are defined, not computed. The strategic viability margin is a supremum over a
       feasible family that cannot be enumerated for a real system, and it presupposes a known objective;
       where the objective is unknown it is NOT EVALUABLE, and no incomplete search confirms a
       positive margin (Section 5.5).


                                                     56
                                      The mathematics of Laws IV and V


     • Identification. The capability elasticity of correction service is the registered correction-leverage
       exponent only under the conditions of Section 4; the local elasticities are identified only where
       capability is assigned or a no-confounding premise is stated in advance (Section 6.4); and the
       identification of the ARC Co-Scaling Law’s 𝛽𝐶 with 𝜒 is unestablished (Section 7.3).
     • Scale. Exponent numbers, P12’s minimum effect among them, hold only on the capability scale
       condition B fixes; signs and verdicts on the balance do not depend on the coordinate, and numbers
       do (Section 7.7).
     • Earlier work. Section 2 names the nearest earlier work found and says what each states. Where this
       report says that a result was not found stated, the search’s date and scope stand with it (Sections 5.9
       and 6.8); it makes no other statement about novelty.
     • Representations. What persists when a rewrite changes the system’s own representations is not
       treated here: the property’s score would then need a map from the successor’s representations to
       the observables it is scored on, one that keeps the property, and how such a map is verified is an
       open problem, named here and not solved.
     • Open questions. Section 10 records fifteen questions; those still open include the budget scale
       and the at-the-margin band for P15, the baseline a unit that decides P15 alone takes and the floor’s
       value, P3’s tolerance, and P12’s listing under conditions B and E2. Law IV, the ARC Persistence
       Law, cannot be supported until instruments decide P15 and P23.


13     Conclusion
Laws IV and V, the ARC Persistence Law and the ARC Embedding Law, are registered laws under test,
and this report gives them their mathematics. The ARC Persistence Law’s retention limb is read on
one quantity, the retained fraction of an externally installed gain at the matched budget, and the report
states what that fraction needs before it can decide P15. Persistence of a property over a finite horizon
follows, under stated premises, from greedy acceptance with a margin or from planned optimisation
with a strategic viability margin, and each premise names the observation that would refute it. Neither
mechanism reaches later training by gradient steps; in the smallest model of that training, an installed gain
fades at the rates the later loss’s curvature sets along it, and, under later training of the same kind, a gain
installed with the capability loss in the objective fades no faster than one installed afterwards. The ARC
Embedding Law’s limbs are read on P12’s interval, with the direction version 1.103 (Eastwood 2026g)
registers, and on P21’s interaction of placement with depth; the balance identity shows how a change
in the capability elasticity the build-order limb reads, or in the direct depth-dependence the placement
limb’s mechanism reading names, would move the boundary inside the ARC Ceiling’s model, which stays
unchanged. In the smallest model of each limb, the placement limb follows where uncorrected faults seed
later ones or an outside checker’s detection falls with what it inspects, and the build-order limb follows
only where what is formed first loses its coverage more slowly than a corrector added after falls behind, so
each model names what its test must measure. None of this is a measurement. The laws take their words
from their registered tests alone: P15’s retained fraction, P23’s difference of retained fractions under later
training of the same kind, P12’s order contrast and P21’s interaction, each read as the registration says.

A     Proofs
The proofs of Lemma IV-K, Theorems IV-G and IV-H, Corollary IV-H′ and Propositions IV-N, IV-R,
V-3, B-7 and B-8, moved here from Sections 5, 6 and 7, where each is stated; and, added in version 1.3,
those of Theorems IV-S and IV-C, Corollary IV-C′ and Propositions IV-L, IV-U, V-4 and V-5. In these
proofs ⟨·⟩ is the mean over the eigen-directions weighted by the external weights 𝜛𝑖 , and a covariance
under those weights is written in its pair form, ⟨𝑋𝑌 ⟩ − ⟨𝑋⟩⟨𝑌 ⟩ = 12 𝑖,𝑖 ′ 𝜛𝑖 𝜛𝑖 ′ (𝑋𝑖 − 𝑋𝑖 ′ ) (𝑌𝑖 − 𝑌𝑖 ′ ), which
                                                                     ∑︁
is non-negative wherever 𝑋 and 𝑌 are similarly ordered (Chebyshev’s sum inequality).

A.1    Proof of Lemma IV-K
Proof of Lemma IV-K. (K1): 𝑇𝐻 (𝑠0 ) is a subset of 𝑇𝐻+1 (𝑠0 ), so if every member of the larger set is safe,
every member of the smaller is. (K2): 𝑇𝐻 (𝑠0 ) under 𝑇 is a subset of 𝑇𝐻 (𝑠0 ) under 𝑇 ′ . (K3): enlarging
the tolerance enlarges Ω𝑉 , so a path safe under the smaller tolerance is safe under the larger. Each is an
inclusion of quantifier ranges and needs nothing about the system.                                        □

A.2    Proof of Theorem IV-G
Proof of Theorem IV-G. Suppose a path of executed transitions from 𝑠0 has a first index 𝑡, with 1 ≤ 𝑡 ≤ 𝐻,
at which 𝑠𝑡 lies outside Ω𝑉 . Then 𝑠𝑡 −1 lies in Ω𝑉 and is reachable within 𝑡 − 1 < 𝐻 accepted transitions.
By (G3) the executed transition 𝑠𝑡 −1 to 𝑠𝑡 lies in 𝑇eval (𝑠𝑡 −1 ), so by (G2) 𝐽 (𝑠𝑡 ) − 𝐽 (𝑠𝑡 −1 ) < 0. But the
transition was executed, so by (G1) 𝐽 (𝑠𝑡 ) − 𝐽 (𝑠𝑡 −1 ) ≥ 0. Contradiction; no such 𝑡 exists. Since 𝑡 ranges
over a finite set, no further condition is needed.                                                             □

A.4    Proof of Corollary IV-H′
Proof of Corollary IV-H′ . By induction on 𝑡: 𝑠0 lies in Ω𝑉 ; if 𝑠𝑡 does, the plan executed from 𝑠𝑡 is safe,
by IV-H where (H1) to (H3) hold and because every feasible plan is safe where the exit part is empty; if it
stops at 𝑠𝑡 the executed path ends there, in Ω𝑉 , and otherwise, by (iii), 𝑠𝑡+1 is a state of a safe plan and
lies in Ω𝑉 .                                                                                                □

A.6    Proof of Proposition IV-R
Proof of Proposition IV-R. Write

                         quad(𝑚) := (𝑌ˆ1 − 𝑚𝑌ˆ0 ) 2 − 𝑧 2crit (var1 − 2𝑚 cov10 + 𝑚 2 var0 ),                     (A.1)

so that Fieller’s set is where quad(𝑚) ≤ 0. It is a quadratic in 𝑚 with leading coefficient 𝑌ˆ02 − 𝑧2crit var0 ,
which the gate makes positive, so the set is a closed bounded interval or is empty. At 𝑚 = 𝑌ˆ1 /𝑌ˆ0 the
squared term vanishes and quad equals minus 𝑧 2crit times an estimated variance, which is not negative; so
quad is not positive there, and the set is an interval containing 𝑌ˆ1 /𝑌ˆ0 . If the interval lies entirely above 𝑚 thr ,
then 𝑚 thr is outside it, so quad(𝑚 thr ) > 0, and 𝑌ˆ1 /𝑌ˆ0 , a point of the interval, exceeds 𝑚 thr . Conversely, if
quad(𝑚 thr ) > 0 and 𝑌ˆ1 /𝑌ˆ0 > 𝑚 thr , then 𝑚 thr is outside the interval and below one of its points, so it is
below all of them. Now quad(𝑚 thr ) > 0 says that the square of 𝑌ˆ1 − 𝑚 thr𝑌ˆ0 exceeds the square of the
right-hand side displayed, and, because 𝑌ˆ0 > 0, 𝑌ˆ1 /𝑌ˆ0 > 𝑚 thr says that 𝑌ˆ1 − 𝑚 thr𝑌ˆ0 is positive; the two
together are the displayed inequality, (7).                                                                         □

A.7    Proof of Theorem IV-S
Proof of Theorem IV-S. Each run follows 𝜔¤ = −Φ(𝜔 − 𝜔★), so 𝜔(𝜏) − 𝜔★ = 𝑒 −Φ𝜏 (𝜔(0) − 𝜔★), and the
treated run less the base run is 𝑒 −Φ𝜏 𝑤, since 𝜔★ cancels. So 𝑌 (𝜏) = 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑒 −Φ𝜏 𝑤 = 𝑖 𝑔𝑖 𝑤 𝑖 𝑒 −𝜆𝑖 𝜏 in the
                                                                                       ∑︁
eigenbasis of Φ; dividing by 𝑌 (0) = 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑤 = 𝑖 𝑔𝑖 𝑤 𝑖 gives equation (8), with weights that sum to one. For
                                              ∑︁
the external arm, 𝑤 is a positive multiple of 𝑔, so 𝜛𝑖 = 𝑔𝑖2 /|𝑔| 2 ≥ 0: every term of 𝑆ext is non-negative
and non-increasing in 𝜏, the terms with 𝜆𝑖 = 0 are constant and sum to 𝜛flat , so 𝑆ext (𝜏) ≥ 𝜛flat , and 𝑆ext
tends to 𝜛flat , falling below one half beyond a finite time where 𝜛flat < 1/2.                            □

A.8    Proof of Proposition IV-L
Proof of Proposition IV-L. The joint objective is the original loss less a positive multiple of 𝑉, and
since only the direction of the displacement matters once the installed effect is matched, the multiple
is taken to be one. Then the displacement 𝑤(𝜏) := 𝜔(𝜏) − 𝜔0 solves 𝑤¤ = −Φ0 𝑤 + 𝑔 with 𝑤(0) = 0,
so 𝑤(𝜏j ) = (𝐼 − 𝑒 −Φ0 𝜏j )Φ0−1 𝑔 where Φ0 is invertible, with the component 𝜏j 𝑔𝑖 along any direction in
which 𝜆0𝑖 = 0; as 𝜏j grows it tends to Φ0−1 𝑔 for Φ0 positive definite. At second order a displacement 𝑤
costs 12 𝑤 ⊤ Φ0 𝑤 of the original loss. The external displacement 𝑌 (0) 𝑔/|𝑔| 2 costs 12 𝑌 (0) 2 (𝑔 ⊤ Φ0 𝑔)/|𝑔| 4 .
Minimising 12 𝑤 ⊤ Φ0 𝑤 subject to 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑤 = 𝑌 (0), the Lagrange condition Φ0 𝑤 parallel to 𝑔 gives 𝑤 emb =
𝑌 (0) Φ0−1 𝑔/(𝑔 ⊤ Φ0−1 𝑔) and the cost 12 𝑌 (0) 2 /(𝑔 ⊤ Φ0−1 𝑔). By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality applied to
Φ1/2

                    𝑔, |𝑔| 4 ≤ (𝑔 ⊤ Φ0 𝑔) (𝑔 ⊤ Φ0−1 𝑔), which is the order of the two costs.                  □

A.9    Proof of Theorem IV-C
Proof of Theorem IV-C. In the shared eigenbasis, 𝑤 emb has components proportional to 𝑔𝑖 /𝜆0𝑖 , so the
embedded weights are proportional to 𝜛𝑖 /𝜆0𝑖 , and 𝑆ext (𝜏) = ⟨𝑒 −𝜆𝜏 ⟩ and 𝑆emb (𝜏) = ⟨𝑒 −𝜆𝜏 /𝜆0 ⟩/⟨1/𝜆0 ⟩,
means over the directions the property depends on. So 𝑆emb (𝜏) ≥ 𝑆ext (𝜏) exactly when the covariance
of 1/𝜆0 and 𝑒 −𝜆𝜏 is non-negative. For 𝜏 > 0 the first decreases in 𝜆0 and the second in 𝜆, so under
Assumption S they are similarly ordered and every term of the pair form is non-negative. The covariance
is zero exactly when, for every pair, 𝜆0𝑖 = 𝜆0𝑖 ′ or 𝜆 𝑖 = 𝜆𝑖 ′ ; if neither were constant, take 𝑖, 𝑖 ′ with
𝜆0𝑖 ≠ 𝜆0𝑖 ′ , so 𝜆𝑖 = 𝜆𝑖 ′ , and 𝑖 ′′ with 𝜆 𝑖 ′′ ≠ 𝜆𝑖 ; then 𝜆0𝑖 ′′ = 𝜆0𝑖 and 𝜆0𝑖 ′′ = 𝜆0𝑖 ′ , a contradiction. For the
ratio, d ln(𝑆emb /𝑆ext )/d𝜏 is the mean of 𝜆 under the weights 𝜛𝑖 𝑒 −𝜆𝑖 𝜏 less its mean under the weights
𝜛𝑖 𝑒 −𝜆𝑖 𝜏 /𝜆0𝑖 ; the second weights are the first tilted by 1/𝜆0 , which under Assumption S is oppositely
ordered to 𝜆, so the covariance form gives the second mean no larger, and the derivative is non-negative. □

A.11     Proof of Proposition IV-U
Proof of Proposition IV-U. (1) The coupled objective is quadratic with Hessian Φ + 𝛾𝑔𝑔 ⊤ , so from its
minimum a change 𝑤 raises it by 12 𝑤 ⊤ (Φ + 𝛾𝑔𝑔 ⊤ )𝑤. Among changes that lower 𝑉 by a given amount the
term 12 𝛾(𝑔 ⊤ 𝑤) 2 is fixed at 12 𝛾 times the amount’s square, and the least of 12 𝑤 ⊤ Φ𝑤 is 12 stf times it: by a
Lagrange multiplier where 𝑔 lies in the range of Φ, and zero, along a null direction 𝑛 of Φ with 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑛 ≠ 0,
where it does not. (2) The minimum 𝜔c satisfies Φ(𝜔c −𝜔★)+𝛾 dev 𝑔 = 0, with dev := 𝑉 (𝜔c )−𝑉tgt . Where
𝑔 lies in the range of Φ, it is orthogonal to the null space, so 𝑉 (𝜔c ) −𝑉 (𝜔★) = −𝛾 dev 𝑔 ⊤ Φ+ 𝑔 = −𝛾 dev/stf,
and dev = (𝑉 (𝜔★) −𝑉tgt ) stf/(stf + 𝛾); where 𝑔 has a component in the null space, projecting the condition
onto that space gives 𝛾 dev times that component equal to zero, so dev = 0. (3) A vector 𝑛 lies in the
null space of Φ + 𝛾𝑔𝑔 ⊤ exactly when 𝑛⊤ Φ𝑛 + 𝛾(𝑔 ⊤ 𝑛) 2 = 0, that is when Φ𝑛 = 0 and 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑛 = 0; so 𝑔
                                                         ⊤
is orthogonal to that null space and 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑒 − (Φ+𝛾𝑔𝑔 ) 𝜏 𝑤 tends to zero for every 𝑤. Without coupling, a
departure along a null direction 𝑛 of Φ with 𝑔 ⊤ 𝑛 ≠ 0 keeps 𝑔⊤ 𝑒 −Φ𝜏 𝑛 = 𝑔⊤ 𝑛.                                 □

A.12     Proof of Proposition V-3
Proof of Proposition V-3. ln 𝑄 𝑜𝑙 − 𝜒𝑜𝑙 ln 𝐶 − 𝜂𝑜𝑙 ln 𝑅 has zero partials, so it is constant on the connected
rectangle; the constant is fixed by the reference point, which must lie in the rectangle; the partials of the
closed form return the constants.                                                                           □

A.13     Proof of Proposition V-4
Proof of Proposition V-4. In the propagation setting a new fault of the in-round arm survives its round with
probability 1−𝛿in , and each surviving fault seeds 𝜎 new faults a round, of which the same fraction survive; so
𝑁in (𝑅) = (1+𝜎(1−𝛿in ))𝑁in (𝑅−1) +𝑞(1−𝛿in ) with 𝑁in (0) = 0, whose solution is equation (14). Without
correction the expected faults 𝑁0 (𝑅) solve 𝑁0 (𝑅) = (1+ 𝜎)𝑁0 (𝑅 −1) + 𝑞, and the outside arm removes the
fraction 𝛿out at the end. (a) At 𝜎 = 0 both counts are linear in 𝑅. (b) The ratio is (1− 𝛿out ) (𝑦 𝑅𝑐 −1)/(𝑦 𝑅 −1)
with 𝑦 𝑅 := (1 + 𝜎(1 − 𝛿in )) 𝑅 , increasing in 𝑅, and 𝑐 := ln(1 + 𝜎)/ln(1 + 𝜎(1 − 𝛿in )) > 1; for 𝑐 > 1
the slope (𝑦 𝑐 − 1)/(𝑦 − 1) of the chord from the point (1, 1) of the convex curve 𝑦 𝑐 increases in
𝑦 > 1, so the ratio rises at every depth, and it grows like (1 − 𝛿out ) [(1 + 𝜎)/(1 + 𝜎(1 − 𝛿in ))] 𝑅 .
With equal detection, adv(1) = (1 − 𝛿)𝑞 − 𝑞(1 − 𝛿) = 0, and the increment from 𝑅 − 1 to 𝑅 is
(1 − 𝛿) [𝑁0 (𝑅) − 𝑁0 (𝑅 − 1)] − [𝑁in (𝑅) − 𝑁in (𝑅 − 1)] = 𝑞(1 − 𝛿) [(1 + 𝜎) 𝑅−1 − (1 + 𝜎(1 − 𝛿)) 𝑅−1 ], positive
for 𝑅 ≥ 2. (c) The ratio rises at every depth by the argument of (b), which needs only 0 < 𝛿in < 1 and a
constant 𝛿out below one. In general the increment is 𝑞 [(1 − 𝛿out ) (1 + 𝜎) 𝑅−1 − (1 − 𝛿in ) (1 + 𝜎(1 − 𝛿in )) 𝑅−1 ],
which is positive exactly when (𝑅 − 1) ln[(1 + 𝜎)/(1 + 𝜎(1 − 𝛿in ))] > ln[(1 − 𝛿in )/(1 − 𝛿out )]; with
0 < 𝛿in the logarithm on the left is positive, and dividing by it gives 𝑅 > 𝑅turn , the increment being
negative when 𝑅 < 𝑅turn . At 𝛿in = 0 that logarithm is zero, the ratio is 1 − 𝛿out at every depth, and the
increment is −𝑞 𝛿out (1 + 𝜎) 𝑅−1 , negative at every depth. (d) A round’s mean score is 1 − 2𝑞(1 − 𝛿in )

A.14     Proof of Proposition V-5
Proof of Proposition V-5. (a) ln 𝑄 is ln mis plus the logarithm of the coverage, and ln mis cancels in the
contrast. The capability elasticity of cvgfirst is 𝑒 co − ℎfirst , and that of cvgafter is −𝑒 rep rep/(𝑛ex + rep),
which gives equation (16). Across two rungs, with constant elasticities, the estimate is 𝑒 co − ℎfirst +
ln[(𝑛ex + rep(𝐶2 ))/(𝑛ex + rep(𝐶1 ))]/ln(𝐶2 /𝐶1 ), and since rep(𝐶2 )/rep(𝐶1 ) = (𝐶2 /𝐶1 ) 𝑒rep ≥ 1, the ratio
in the logarithm lies between one and (𝐶2 /𝐶1 ) 𝑒rep , so the last term lies between zero and 𝑒 rep . (b) The
last term of equation (16) is non-negative. (c) With 𝑛ex > 0, that term is below 𝑒 rep where 𝑒 rep > 0 and
zero where 𝑒 rep = 0, and the two-rung term likewise; so both are at most 𝑒 co − ℎfirst + 𝑒 rep ≤ 0, and
negative where ℎfirst > 𝑒 co . (d) With ret(𝐶) = 𝑆(𝜏(𝐶)), −d ln ret/d ln 𝐶 = (−d ln 𝑆/d𝜏) (d𝜏/d ln 𝐶). (e)
Theorem IV-C says that ln(𝑆emb /𝑆ext ) does not fall in 𝜏, so the embedded installation’s log-decay rate is
at most the external one’s at every 𝜏; with the same map from time to capability, (d) gives it the smaller
ℎfirst , and equation (16) the larger contrast. (f) Equation (16) depends on the size of ℎfirst and not on how
two installations compare, so a refuted comparison fixes neither the size of the decay nor the other terms,
and a contrast of any size fixes no comparison.                                                                 □

A.15     Moments of the error carried across rewrites
The moments and the convergence. Without the pull, 𝑉 (𝑛) − 𝑉 (0) = 𝑡=1            err𝑡 , a sum of 𝑛 independent
                                                                          ∑︁𝑛
terms, with mean 𝑛 bias and variance 𝑛 varerr . With it, the departure dep𝑛 := 𝑉 (𝑛) − 𝑉 (0) obeys dep𝑛 =
(1 − pull) dep𝑛−1 + err𝑛 with dep0 = 0, so dep𝑛 = 𝑡=1    (1 − pull) 𝑛−𝑡 err𝑡 ; its mean is bias 𝑛−1𝑗=0 (1 − pull)
                                                     ∑︁𝑛                                        ∑︁                𝑗

and its variance varerr 𝑗=0 (1 − pull) 2 𝑗 , the two geometric sums stated, each rising with 𝑛 to its limit.
                       ∑︁𝑛−1
For independent errors whose variances have a finite sum, the partial sums of the centred errors form a
martingale bounded in mean square, which converges almost surely; so the sum of the errors converges
exactly where the sum of their means converges.                                                                  □

A.16     Proof of Proposition B-7
Proof of Proposition B-7. 𝜒 in the new coordinate is the derivative of ln 𝑄 in the new log-capability at
fixed 𝑅, which is 𝜒 times the derivative of the old log-capability in the new, the reciprocal of the map’s
elasticity, and likewise 𝜅; 𝜂 and 𝜁 are taken at fixed capability, which the new coordinate fixes exactly
when the old one does; 𝛼𝑅 is multiplied by the elasticity by the chain rule; and Δbal = d ln(𝑄/𝑊)/d ln 𝑅
along the path names no capability coordinate.                                                          □

A.17     Proof of Proposition B-8
Proof of Proposition B-8. By Identity B-1, Δbal is the derivative of ln(𝑄/𝑊) in ln 𝑅 along the trajectory;
integrate it from 𝑅1 to 𝑅2 .                                                                            □

B.1    Version 1.4, 22 November 2026
Version 1.4 is the report’s next version. It follows version 1.3 of 17 November 2026, which stays published
as revision 3 of the report’s PDF on OSF.

Why it was made. Version 1.105 of the theory registration, approved on OSF on 18 November 2026,
registers P23, the entry of the comparison limb of Law IV, the ARC Persistence Law, so that version
1.3’s account of the registered setting no longer matched the registration. Outside reviews read on 21
November 2026, each point checked at its source within the programme, found two results stated without
a premise or a range they need, one connection to Law II, the ARC Co-Scaling Law, made through the
exponent of absolute growth where Paper X’s criterion uses that of fractional growth, and one limit of a
frozen design that the report did not state; notes the author sent the same week raised questions about
persistence across rewrites. Version 1.4 carries every verified fix in one version.

What changed.
    1. The registration at version 1.105. P23 is printed where the report states Law IV, the ARC
       Persistence Law, and what each register carries (Sections 3.1, 3.2, 5.1 and 11, and Table 1). The
       comparison limb takes P23’s verdict, and the law can be supported only when P15 and P23 are
       both supported (Section 5.8); P23’s baseline, three arms and capability rise are recorded where
       the report states the measurement (Sections 5.6 and 5.6.1); a refuted P23 is the verdict that strikes

                                    The mathematics of Laws IV and V


     Law V, the ARC Embedding Law, through its registered dependence (Sections 6.6 and 8); P23
     is a fifth carrier of the Eden Protocol (Section 8, item 5); the open questions the registration now
     settles in part are marked (Section 10, items 1, 3, 8, 9 and 15); and the registration’s resolution of
     the two statements of the build-order limb’s refutation is recorded (Section 6.6, item 2). Wherever
     the report gives a registered rule’s words it now cites them where version 1.105 states them; a dated
     statement of what an earlier version held keeps that version.
  2. The weak ordering. Section 5.9 now says that Theorem IV-C orders the two arms only weakly,
     with equality where either curvature is the same in every direction the property depends on, and that
     P23’s margin of 0.10 is the registration’s empirical prediction and no consequence of the theorem.
  3. Proposition V-4. Part (c) now requires 0 < 𝛿in , which it needs: at 𝛿in = 0 the ratio is constant,
     there is no turning depth and the difference falls at every depth, as the part now says; its proof and
     the notation entry carry the condition. The arrivals setting now bounds the score of an entry kept
     out, −1 ≤ 𝑦 k ≤ 1, which the reading of part (e) needs: outside that range a falling rejection of
     sound work can widen the lead (Section 6.8).
  4. Section 7.3. Version 1.3 connected the criterion of Law II, the ARC Co-Scaling Law, to the ceiling
     of Law III, the ARC Ceiling, by giving the burden model’s exponent (𝛼 − 1)/𝛼 as the 𝑘 of Paper X,
     whose 𝑘 is the exponent of fractional growth. On the depth clock that exponent is −1/𝛼, the relative
     criterion does not give the ceiling, and the reciprocal form comes from Paper X’s absolute criterion;
     the section now says so, places the registration’s drift exponent as that 𝑘 plus one, and states the
     rule for a change of clock. The identification 𝛽𝐶 = 𝜒 stays unestablished.
  5. Unit 83’s monitor. Section 6.5 states one limit of the frozen design of Unit 83, the counted test of
     in-loop correction with depth, that bears on reading its widening, with the descriptive split that
     Unit 83b’s registration records.
  6. The label. The label UNREGISTERED is now written (the report’s own models; not registered)
     after a name, and in a numbered result’s title after the name inside the title’s own parentheses, as
     Section 9 defines once; the meaning is unchanged.
  7. Weighed and added. Section 5.5.1 models error carried across rewrites, a random walk where
     each rewrite is judged by the values then held and a bounded departure where rewrites are pulled
     back towards a retained reference, with its derivation in Appendix A.15; Section 6.4 rules that a
     design with a handful of model families treats family as a fixed stratum; the list of Section 7.9 asks
     for the reason that excludes power re-expressions of capability, the balance exponent beside every
     number stated on the scale and the readings under alternative scales; and Section 12 names, as an
     open problem, what persists when a rewrite changes the system’s representations.
  8. Earlier work. Five passages now say only what the parts of the works read carry. In Section 2,
     one measured advantage is said to widen with the number of sampled solutions searched, not with
     depth, and two works that could not be read at source are credited with what their titles name, as
     their references now say; two passages that named the source of the ratio interval where the report
     states its own result now name the interval alone (Sections 5.6.1 and 9), the source’s reference
     saying that its abstract was read.
  9. The first page. The abstract states P23’s prediction beside P15’s, within its limit.
 10. What the embedding mathematics does not say. Section 6.7 adds one more thing the mathematics
     does not say: that embedding a model of harm makes its use for avoidance hard to invert.

      Corollary IV-H′ starts from a state in Ω𝑉 (Section 5.5); the constant two of Proposition IV-N cannot

      critical value is at least the gate’s (Section 5.6.1); and the boundary reading holds for changes that
      keep 𝜅 − 𝜒 of one sign (Section 6.1).

      a concurrent-baseline retained fraction reads a property the later objective holds as a gain lost
      (Section 5.6); a dilution common to both arms shrinks the difference and leaves Λ𝑉 unchanged
      (Section 9); the registration’s two statements of the build-order limb’s refutation differ on some
      intervals (Section 6.6, item 2, and Section 10, item 15); on the counted instrument for P21 the route
      of recursion as such is absent by design (Section 6.5); equation (22) reads 𝜒 as an elasticity of
      correction capacity (Section 7.6); and the third of Section 1’s plain terms names the two quantities
      that move the balance root, a correction the conclusion carries too.

      premise the build-order limb carries. The proofs are in Appendix A.

      which embedded loops fare no better than removed enforcement kill ARC-4, and ARC-5
      with it.” That sentence is comparative and its kill is comparative; neither is P15’s registered
      face, and K-IV below reaches neither. Whether Law IV carries the comparative content,
      with a registered comparative refuter and instrument, or P15’s face alone, is the author’s first
      decision (section 7).
